]
do not discuss.

Proof.

The generic values of
c
for
t=0
are
c\neq 0
and for
t=1
they are
c\notin\mathbb{F}_{p}
by Proposition
2.16
. For those values,
[
EG
, Thm 1.7]
and
[
BFG
, Rmk 1.2.3]
(see also proof of Prop 4.1. and 4.2. in
[
DSa
]
) imply that
\dim L_{0,c}(\tau)=|S_{3}|=6
, and
\dim L_{1,c}(\tau)=|S_{3}|\cdot\dim S^{(p)}\mathfrak{h}^{*}=6p^{2}
. For
t=0,1
and
\tau=\mathrm{Triv},\mathrm{Sign}
, this is equal to the dimension of
N_{t,c}(\tau)
, whose characters have been computed in Corollary
2.7
. This implies that
L_{t,c}(\tau)=N_{t,c}(\tau)
and proves the theorem for
\tau=\mathrm{Triv},\mathrm{Sign}
.

When
\tau=\mathrm{Stand}
, the same reasoning tells us that
L_{t,c}(\tau)
is a proper quotient of
N_{t,c}(\tau)
.

For
t=0
the additional singular vectors are computed in Lemma
3.7
, and the module
L_{0,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
and its character described in Lemma
3.8
. We rewrite them in the basis
b_{+},b_{-}
in Remark
6.2
. This proves the theorem for
t=0
,
\tau=\mathrm{Stand}
.

For
t=1
the additional singular vectors are computed in Lemma
6.7
, and the character of the quotient of
N_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
by these vectors is computed in Lemma
6.14
. Observing the dimension of this module is equal to
6p^{2}
we conclude it is irreducible and thus equal to
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
, which proves the theorem for
t=1
,
\tau=\mathrm{Stand}
.

∎

Remark 6.2
.

Let
\tau=\mathrm{Stand}
. For
t=1
the space of additional singular vectors
\mathrm{span}\{v_{+},v_{-}\}
is described in terms
v_{+},v_{-}
written in the basis
b_{+},b_{-}
in Lemma
6.7
. For
t=0
we do not do the analogue because the space of additional singular vectors is already described in terms of
v_{1},v_{2}
in Lemmas
3.7
and
3.8
. However, an analogous computation would yield
v_{+},v_{-}\in M_{0,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
, related to
v_{1},v_{2}
via

\displaystyle v_{+}

\displaystyle=-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-}=-6\pi(v_{1}+v_{2})

\displaystyle v_{-}

\displaystyle=b_{+}\otimes b_{-}+b_{-}\otimes b_{+}=-2\pi(v_{1}-v_{2}).

6.1.
Singular vectors in
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})

The rest of the section is dedicated to the remaining case of
t=1
,
\tau=\mathrm{Stand}
and generic
c
. We will describe the generators of
J_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
by explicit computations of Dunkl operators in a basis from Theorem
2.6
.

By
[
BCb
, Prop 3.4]
, for generic
c
singular vectors only appear in degrees divisible by
p
. By Lemma
2.1
, any singular vectors in degrees divisible by
p
of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
are in the isotypic component of
\mathrm{Stand}
. As
\mathrm{Stand}
is irreducible, it is enough to look for singular vectors in the one dimensional subspace of
\mathrm{Stand}
which restricts to the trivial representation of
S_{2}
(in other words, the image of
b_{+}
under any isomorphism from
\mathrm{Stand}
).
By Theorem
2.6
, a basis of this part of
\mathrm{Stand}
in degree
kp
is:

•

\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\otimes b_{+}
,
2a+3b=kp
;

•

\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})
,
2a+3b=kp-1
;

•

\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot\left(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)
,
2a+3b=kp-2
;

•

\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}q\otimes b_{-}
,
2a+3b=kp-3
.

By Lemma
2.20
, looking for singular vectors in degree
kp
means looking for a linear combination of the above vectors which is in the kernel of
D_{y_{1}}
. We first calculate the values of the Dunkl operator
D_{y_{1}}
on the vectors listed above.

Lemma 6.3
.

(1)

Let
a,b,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
2a+3b=kp
. Then

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\otimes b_{+})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-a}{6}\right)\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b}\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{+}\right)+

\displaystyle\enskip+\left(\frac{b}{36}\right)\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}\right).

(2)

Let
a,b,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
2a+3b=kp-1
. Then

\displaystyle\hskip-30.0ptD_{y_{1}}(-\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}b_{-}\otimes b_{-})=\hskip 30.0pt

\displaystyle\quad=\left(\frac{-1}{2}\right)\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\otimes\left(b_{+}-3b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{-b}{6}\right)\sigma_{2}^{a+1}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}-3b_{-}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{+}\otimes b_{-}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{-a}{12}\right)\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b}\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}-3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}+\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt\left.+3(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{-b}{2}\right)\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}q\otimes\left(b_{+}+b_{-}\right).

(3)

Let
a,b,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
2a+3b=kp-2
. Then

\displaystyle\hskip-30.0ptD_{y_{1}}(-\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-})=

\displaystyle=\enskip(-2b)\sigma_{2}^{a+2}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\otimes(b_{+}-3b_{-})+

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+(9a)\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b+1}\otimes(b_{+}-3b_{-})+

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+(-a)\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}-3b_{-}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{+}\otimes b_{-}+3b_{+}\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+(18c)\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\left(\frac{b}{6}\right)\sigma_{2}^{a+1}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}-3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}+\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt\left.+3(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+(-3a)\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b}q\otimes\left(b_{+}+b_{-}\right).

(4)

Let
a,b,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
2a+3b=kp-3
. Then

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}q\otimes b_{-})

\displaystyle=c\sigma_{2}^{a+1}\sigma_{3}^{b}\otimes\left(b_{+}-3b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\enskip+\frac{b}{3}\sigma_{2}^{a+2}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\enskip+\frac{-3a}{2}\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b+1}\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\enskip+\frac{-c}{12}\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}-3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}+\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt\left.+3(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right).

Proof.

Direct computation using the auxiliary results from Section
5
.
∎

Let us first look for singular vectors in degree
p
first (this will turn out to be enough).
Parametrising the integers
a,b
which label the basis from the start of this section like in the proof of Lemma
2.3
, we are looking for a vector in degree
p
of the form

(8)

\displaystyle v_{+}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor}\alpha_{j}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}\otimes b_{+}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-1}{6}\rfloor}\beta_{j}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}\cdot(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-5}{6}\rfloor}\gamma_{j}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-5}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}\left(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor}\delta_{j}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}\cdot q\otimes b_{-}

for some
\alpha_{j},\beta_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j}\in\Bbbk
(depending on
c
) with the property that
D_{y_{1}}(v_{+})=0
.

Lemma 6.4
.

If a vector
v_{+}
of the form (
8
) satisfies
D_{y_{1}}(v_{+})=0
, then

(1)

\alpha_{j}=0
for all
j
;

(2)

For all
0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-7}{6}\rfloor

\gamma_{j}=\frac{2(j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}.

Note that if
p\equiv 2\pmod{3}
then there is also a coefficient
\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}
, which this Lemma puts no conditions on. Otherwise this Lemma determines all
\alpha_{j},\gamma_{j}
in terms of
\beta_{j},\delta_{j}
.

Proof.

The right hand sides in each of the expressions in Lemma
6.3
are linearly independent, so we will be reading off their coefficients in the expansion of the equation
D_{y_{1}}(v_{+})=0
using Lemma
6.3
.

(1)

The coefficient of
\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{+}\right)
in the equation
D_{y_{1}}(v_{+})=0
expanded as in Lemma
6.3
equals

\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor}\alpha_{j}\left(-\frac{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j}{6}\right)\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j-1}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}=0.

This tells us that
\alpha_{j}=0
for all
0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor
, except maybe for
j
such that

\frac{p-3}{2}-3j=0,\quad 0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor.

Keeping in mind that the equation
\frac{p-3}{2}-3j=0
is in
\Bbbk
but the inequality
0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor
is in
\mathbb{N}_{0}
, this is equivalent to

j=\frac{p-3}{6}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

However, this means that

6|p-3
so
3|p,

which is impossible as
p
is a prime and
p>3
. In conclusion, no such
j
exists, so
\alpha_{j}=0
for all
j
.

(2)

The coefficient of
q\otimes\left(b_{+}+b_{-}\right)
in the equation
D_{y_{1}}(v_{+})=0
expanded as in Lemma
6.3
equals

\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-1}{6}\rfloor}\beta_{j}\left(\frac{-2j}{2}\right)\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j-1}+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-5}{6}\rfloor}\gamma_{j}(-3)\left(\frac{p-5}{2}-3j\right)\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-5}{2}-3j-1}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}=0.

We rewrite this as

-\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-7}{6}\rfloor}\beta_{j+1}\left(j+1\right)\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-7}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}+\left(\frac{-3}{2}\right)\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-5}{6}\rfloor}\gamma_{j}\left(p-5-6j\right)\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-7}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}=0.

The boundaries of these two sums are different precisely when there exists an integer
j
with
\frac{p-7}{6}<j\leq\frac{p-5}{6}
, which is equivalent to
6j=p-6
(impossible as then
6|p
and
p
is prime) or
6j=p-5
(happens exactly when
p\equiv 2\pmod{3}
). In that case, the coefficient of
\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}
is zero, so for any
p
the above equation becomes

\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-7}{6}\rfloor}\left(-\beta_{j+1}\left(j+1\right)+\left(\frac{-3}{2}\right)\gamma_{j}\left(p-5-6j\right)\right)\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-7}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}=0.

This means that for all
0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-7}{6}\rfloor
we have

\gamma_{j}=\frac{2(j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}.

∎

We use the notation
\delta_{p\equiv 2\pmod{3}}
for the delta function. Using Lemma
6.4
,
any vector
v_{+}
of the form (
8
) which satisfies
D_{y_{1}}(v_{+})=0
is of the form

(9)

\displaystyle v_{+}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-1}{6}\rfloor}\beta_{j}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}\cdot(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-7}{6}\rfloor}\frac{2(j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-5}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}\left(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle+\delta_{p\equiv 2\bmod 3}\cdot\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-2}{3}}\left(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor}\delta_{j}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}\cdot q\otimes b_{-}.

Next, we want to establish the conditions on
\beta_{j}
,
\delta_{j}
, and (where needed)
\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}
.

Lemma 6.5
.

For a vector
v_{+}
of the form (
(II)
) the condition
D_{y_{1}}(v_{+})=0
is equivalent to the following systems of equations:

(1)

If
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
:

For all
0\leq j\leq\frac{p-1}{6}-1

(I)

-\frac{6j+1}{2}\beta_{j}-\frac{4(j+1)(2j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}+c\delta_{j}=0

For all
0\leq j\leq\frac{p-1}{6}-2

(II)

\frac{12(j+1)c}{(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}+\frac{2(j+1)}{3}\delta_{j+1}+\frac{3(6j+3)}{4}\delta_{j}=0,

(II’)

c\cdot\beta_{\frac{p-1}{6}}-3\delta_{\frac{p-1}{6}-1}=0.

(2)

If
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
:

For all
0\leq j\leq\frac{p-5}{6}-1

(I)

-\frac{6j+1}{2}\beta_{j}-\frac{4(j+1)(2j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}+c\delta_{j}=0

(I’)

2\beta_{\frac{p-5}{6}}+\frac{4}{3}\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}+c\delta_{\frac{p-5}{6}}=0

For all
0\leq j\leq\frac{p-5}{6}-1

(II)

\frac{12(j+1)c}{(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}+\frac{2(j+1)}{3}\delta_{j+1}+\frac{3(6j+3)}{4}\delta_{j}=0

(II’)

\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}\cdot 18c+\frac{-3}{2}\delta_{\frac{p-5}{6}}=0

Proof.

We look at all the right hand sides of the values of Dunkl operators on basis vectors from Lemma
6.3
, and setting their coefficients in
D_{y_{1}}(v_{+})
to be
0
get the following equations:

(I)

From the coefficient of
\otimes(b_{+}-3b_{-})
:

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-1}{6}\rfloor}(\frac{-1}{2})\beta_{j}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-7}{6}\rfloor}\frac{2(j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}\cdot(-2)(2j+1)\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-5}{2}-3j+2}\sigma_{3}^{2j}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-7}{6}\rfloor}\frac{2(j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}\cdot 9(\frac{p-5}{2}-3j)\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-5}{2}-3j-1}\sigma_{3}^{2j+2}+

\displaystyle+\delta_{p\equiv 2\bmod 3}\cdot\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}(-2)(\frac{p-2}{3})\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-2}{3}-1}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor}\delta_{j}\cdot c\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j+1}\sigma_{3}^{2j}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-1}{6}\rfloor}(\frac{-1}{2})\beta_{j}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-7}{6}\rfloor}\frac{-4(j+1)(2j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-1}{6}\rfloor}(-3j)\beta_{j}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}+\delta_{p\equiv 2\bmod 3}\cdot\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}\frac{(-2)(p-2)}{3}\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2\cdot\frac{p-5}{6}}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor}\delta_{j}\cdot c\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}.

We now distinguish two cases:

(1)

If
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
, then
\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor=\frac{p-7}{6}
,
\delta_{p\equiv 2\bmod 3}=0
. Reading the coefficient of
\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}
we get that the above equation is equivalent to requiring that for all
0\leq j\leq\frac{p-7}{6}

-\frac{6j+1}{2}\beta_{j}+\frac{-4(j+1)(2j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}+c\delta_{j}=0.

For
j=\frac{p-1}{6}
the coefficient of
\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}
gives
0=0
, which is always satisfied.

(2)

If
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
, then
\lfloor\frac{p-1}{6}\rfloor=\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor=\frac{p-5}{6}
,
\lfloor\frac{p-7}{6}\rfloor=\frac{p-5}{6}-1
and
\delta_{p\equiv 2\bmod 3}=1
. Reading the coefficient of
\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}
we get that the above equation is equivalent to requiring that for all
0\leq j\leq\frac{p-5}{6}-1

\frac{-(6j+1)}{2}\beta_{j}+\frac{-4(j+1)(2j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}+c\delta_{j}=0.

For
j=\frac{p-5}{6}
the coefficient of
\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2j}
gives one additional equation

\frac{-p+4}{2}\beta_{j}+\frac{-2}{3}(p-2)\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}+c\delta_{\frac{p-5}{6}}=0.

Using that
p=0\in\Bbbk
we get exactly the equations (I) and (I’) from the Lemma.

(II)

From the coefficient of
(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{-})
:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-7}{6}\rfloor}\frac{2(j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}\cdot 18c\cdot\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-5}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}+\delta_{p\equiv 2\bmod 3}\cdot\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}\cdot 18c\cdot\sigma_{3}^{2\frac{p-5}{6}+1}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-9}{6}\rfloor}\delta_{j+1}\frac{2(j+1)}{3}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-5}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\lfloor\frac{p-3}{6}\rfloor}\delta_{j}\frac{-3(\frac{p-3}{2}-3j)}{2}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-5}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}.

We again distinguish two cases:

(1)

If
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
, then for all
0\leq j\leq\frac{p-1}{6}-2

\frac{12(j+1)c}{(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}+\frac{2(j+1)}{3}\delta_{j+1}+\frac{3(6j+3)}{4}\delta_{j}=0,

and for
j=\frac{p-1}{6}-1
we get an additional equation

c\cdot\beta_{\frac{p-1}{6}}-3\delta_{\frac{p-1}{6}-1}=0.

(2)

If
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
, then for all
0\leq j\leq\frac{p-5}{6}-1

\frac{12(j+1)c}{(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}+\frac{2(j+1)}{3}\delta_{j+1}+\frac{3(6j+3)}{4}\delta_{j}=0,

and for
j=\frac{p-5}{6}
we get an additional equation

\gamma_{\frac{p-5}{6}}\cdot 18c+\frac{-3}{2}\delta_{\frac{p-5}{6}}=0.

This gives us the equations (II) and (II’) from the Lemma.

(III)

The coefficient of
b_{+}\otimes b_{+}-3b_{-}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{+}\otimes b_{-}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-}
in
D_{y_{1}}(v_{+})=0
is always
0
when
v_{+}
is of the form (
(II)
).

(IV)

The coefficient of
(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}-3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}+3(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}
is
0
precisely when conditions (I) and (I’) are satisfied.

∎

Lemma 6.6
.

For every
p>3
, the system of equations from the statement of Lemma
6.5
has a unique solution up to overall scaling.

Proof.

(1)

If
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
and we write
p=6k+1
, then the unknowns are
\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{k}
and
\delta_{0},\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{k-1}
. Ordering them as

\beta_{k},\delta_{k-1},\beta_{k-1},\ldots,\delta_{0},\beta_{0},

we can treat equations (I),(II),(II’) as recursions that let us calculate each unknown from the previous two. More precisely, choose an arbitrary nonzero
\beta_{k}
, then use (II’) to calculate

\delta_{k-1}=\frac{c}{3}\cdot\beta_{k}.

After that, alternating (I) and (II) lets us calculate the remaining unknowns recursively:

\delta_{j}=\frac{-4}{3(6j+3)}\left(\frac{12(j+1)c}{(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}+\frac{2(j+1)}{3}\delta_{j+1}\right),

\beta_{j}=\frac{2}{6j+1}\left(\frac{-4(j+1)(2j+1)}{3(6j+5)}\beta_{j+1}+c\delta_{j}\right).

(2)

If
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
and we write
p=6k+5
, then the unknowns are
\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{k}
,
\gamma_{k}
, and
\delta_{0},\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{k}
. We choose an arbitrary nonzero
\gamma_{k}
,
use (II’) to calculate

\delta_{k}=12c\cdot\gamma_{k}

and (I’) to calculate

\beta_{k}=-\frac{2}{3}\gamma_{k}-\frac{c}{2}\delta_{k}.

After that, alternating (I) and (II) lets us calculate the remaining unknowns recursively, for
0\leq j\leq k-1
in decreasing order of
j
, as

\delta_{j}=\frac{-16(j+1)c}{3(6j+5)(2j+1)}\beta_{j+1}-\frac{8(j+1)}{27(2j+1)}\delta_{j+1}

\beta_{j}=\frac{-8(j+1)(2j+1)}{3(5+6j)(6j+1)}\beta_{j+1}+\frac{2c}{6j+1}\delta_{j}.

∎

Summarising, we get the following:

Lemma 6.7
.

For every
p>3
and
c\notin\mathbb{F}_{p}
, the space of singular vectors in
M^{p}_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
is two dimensional, spanned by
v_{+},v_{-}
, where
v_{+}
is of the form (
(II)
) with coefficients satisfying the equations from Lemma
6.5
, and
v_{-}=\frac{2}{3}(s_{2}+\frac{1}{2}).v_{+}.

Lemma (
(II)
) only determines
v_{+}
up to overalll scaling, exhibited by the choice of
\beta_{k}
or
\gamma_{k}
at the start of the recursion. Without loss of generality those
\beta_{k}
or
\gamma_{k}
can be chosen as nonzero constants for every
p
, so that they don’t depend on
c
. The other coefficients will then be polynomials in
c
.

Example 6.8
.

When
p=5
a solution is

\gamma_{0}=1,\delta_{0}=2c,\beta_{0}=1-c^{2},

leading to

\displaystyle v_{+}

\displaystyle=(1-c^{2})\sigma_{2}^{2}\cdot(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle+\sigma_{3}\left(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)+2c\sigma_{2}\cdot q\otimes b_{-},

\displaystyle v_{-}

\displaystyle=(1-c^{2})\sigma_{2}^{2}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}+b_{-}\otimes b_{+})+

\displaystyle+\sigma_{3}\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}+(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}\right)+c\sigma_{2}\cdot q\otimes b_{+}.

Example 6.9
.

When
p=7
a solution is

\beta_{1}=2,\,\,\delta_{0}=3c,\,\,\beta_{0}=6c^{2}-2

leading to

\displaystyle v_{+}

\displaystyle=\left((6c^{2}-2)\sigma_{2}^{3}+2\sigma_{3}^{2}\right)\cdot(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle+4\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-5}{2}}\sigma_{3}\left(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle+3c\sigma_{2}^{2}\cdot q\otimes b_{-},

\displaystyle v_{-}

\displaystyle=\left((6c^{2}-2)\sigma_{2}^{3}+2\sigma_{3}^{2}\right)\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}+b_{-}\otimes b_{+})+

\displaystyle+4\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-5}{2}}\sigma_{3}\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}+(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}\right)+

\displaystyle-c\sigma_{2}^{2}\cdot q\otimes b_{-}.

Example 6.10
.

When
p=11
a solution is

\gamma_{1}=3,\,\,\delta_{1}=3c,\,\,\beta_{1}=4c^{2}+9,\,\,\delta_{0}=c(6c^{2}+1),\,\,\beta_{0}=c^{4}+5c^{2}+4

leading to

\displaystyle v_{+}

\displaystyle=\left((c^{4}+5c^{2}+4)\sigma_{2}^{5}+(4c^{2}+9)\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}\right)\cdot(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle+(2c^{2}-1)\sigma_{2}^{3}\sigma_{3}\left(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)

\displaystyle+c(6c^{2}+1)\sigma_{2}^{4}\cdot q\otimes b_{-}+3c\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}\cdot q\otimes b_{-},

\displaystyle v_{-}

\displaystyle=\left((c^{4}+5c^{2}+4)\sigma_{2}^{5}+(4c^{2}+9)\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}\right)\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}+b_{-}\otimes b_{+})+

\displaystyle+(2c^{2}-1)\sigma_{2}^{3}\sigma_{3}\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}+(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}\right)

\displaystyle-4c(6c^{2}+1)\sigma_{2}^{4}\cdot q\otimes b_{+}-c\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}\cdot q\otimes b_{+}.

Example 6.11
.

When
p=13
a solution is

\beta_{2}=3,\delta_{1}=c,\beta_{1}=9+4c^{2},\delta_{0}=c(7c^{2}-2),\beta_{0}=c^{4}+6c^{2}+3,

leading to

\displaystyle v_{+}

\displaystyle=\left((c^{4}+6c^{2}+3)\sigma_{2}^{6}+(9+4c^{2})\sigma_{2}^{3}\sigma_{3}^{2}+3\sigma_{3}^{4}\right)\cdot(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle+\left((9+4c^{2})\sigma_{2}^{4}\sigma_{3}-2\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}^{3}\right)\cdot\left(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle+\left(c(7c^{2}-2)\sigma_{2}^{5}+c\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}\right)\cdot q\otimes b_{-},

\displaystyle v_{-}

\displaystyle=\left((c^{4}+6c^{2}+3)\sigma_{2}^{6}+(9+4c^{2})\sigma_{2}^{3}\sigma_{3}^{2}+3\sigma_{3}^{4}\right)\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}+b_{-}\otimes b_{+})+

\displaystyle+\left((9+4c^{2})\sigma_{2}^{4}\sigma_{3}-2\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}^{3}\right)\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}+(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}\right)+

\displaystyle+\frac{-1}{3}\left(c(7c^{2}-2)\sigma_{2}^{5}+c\sigma_{2}^{2}\sigma_{3}^{2}\right)\cdot q\otimes b_{+}.

6.2.
Calculating a determinant

The previous section shows that the space of singular vectors in
M^{p}_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
is two dimensional, spanned by
v_{+},v_{-}
from Lemma
6.7
, and isomorphic to
\mathrm{Stand}
via
b_{\pm}\mapsto v_{\pm}
.
We would now like to understand the submodule generated by
v_{+},v_{-}
, using an argument similar to that in the proof of Lemma
3.8
.

Let us name their components as

v_{+}=a_{++}\otimes b_{+}+a_{+-}\otimes b_{-}

v_{-}=a_{-+}\otimes b_{+}+a_{--}\otimes b_{-}.

The determinant that we will have to compute to understand the submodule
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
is

Det=a_{++}a_{--}-a_{+-}a_{-+}\in S^{2p}\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

Proposition 6.12
.

(1)

Det
is an invariant in degree
2p
.

(2)

Det
is in the kernel of
\partial_{y_{1}}
.

(3)

Det
is of the form
Det=f(c)\cdot\sigma_{2}^{p}
, where
f(c)\in\Bbbk[c]
is a polynomial in
c
.

Proof.

The proof does not use the explicit form of
v_{+}
from the previous subsection; everything follows from the conditions

D_{y_{1}}v_{+}=D_{y_{1}}v_{-}=0,\quad s_{1}.v_{+}=v_{+},s_{1}.v_{-}=-v_{-},\quad v_{-}=\frac{2}{3}(s_{2}+\frac{1}{2}).v_{+}.

(1)

The facts that

s_{1}.v_{+}=v_{+},s_{1}.v_{-}=-v_{-}

imply that

s_{1}.a_{++}=a_{++},\quad s_{1}.a_{+-}=-a_{+-},\quad s_{1}.a_{-+}=-a_{-+},\quad s_{1}.a_{--}=a_{--}.

From this immediately follows that

\displaystyle s_{1}.Det

\displaystyle=a_{++}a_{--}-(-a_{+-})(-a_{-+})=Det.

To prove the
s_{2}
invariance of
Det
, we expand

v_{-}=\frac{2}{3}(s_{2}+\frac{1}{2})v_{+}

to get

(10)

\displaystyle a_{-+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\bigg(-(s_{2}.a_{++})+(s_{2}.a_{+-})+a_{++}\bigg)

\displaystyle a_{--}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\bigg(3(s_{2}.a_{++})+(s_{2}.a_{+-})+a_{+-}\bigg)

We can now rewrite
Det
as:

\displaystyle Det

\displaystyle=a_{++}(s_{2}.a_{++})+\frac{1}{3}a_{++}(s_{2}.a_{+-})+\frac{1}{3}a_{+-}(s_{2}.a_{++})-\frac{1}{3}a_{+-}(s_{2}.a_{+-}),

making it clear that it is an
s_{2}
invariant.

(2)

We can use formulas (
10
) to deduce:

(11)

\displaystyle(s_{2}.a_{++})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\bigg(a_{++}-a_{+-}-3a_{-+}+3a_{--}\bigg)

\displaystyle(s_{2}.a_{+-})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\bigg(-3a_{++}-a_{+-}+9a_{-+}+3a_{--}\bigg).

Similarly, the formula for the action of
s_{2}
on
v_{-}
gives us:

(12)

\displaystyle(s_{2}.a_{-+})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\bigg(-a_{++}+a_{+-}-a_{-+}+a_{--}\bigg)

\displaystyle(s_{2}.a_{--})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\bigg(3a_{++}+a_{+-}+3a_{-+}+a_{--}\bigg).

From this we can also easily deduce formulas for
(13).a_{++}
etc.

As we know the action of the group on all
a_{\pm\pm}
, we can expand
D_{y_{1}}(v_{\pm})=0
to get

(13)

\displaystyle\partial_{1}(a_{++})

\displaystyle=\frac{-c}{2(x_{1}-x_{3})}\bigg(a_{+-}-3a_{-+}\bigg)

\displaystyle\partial_{1}(a_{+-})

\displaystyle=\frac{-2c}{x_{1}-x_{2}}a_{+-}+\frac{c}{2(x_{1}-x_{3})}\bigg(-3a_{++}+2a_{+-}+3a_{--}\bigg)

\displaystyle\partial_{1}(a_{-+})

\displaystyle=\frac{2c}{x_{1}-x_{2}}a_{-+}-\frac{c}{2(x_{1}-x_{3})}\bigg(-a_{++}+2a_{-+}+a_{--}\bigg)

\displaystyle\partial_{1}(a_{--})

\displaystyle=\frac{c}{2(x_{1}-x_{3})}\bigg(a_{+-}-3a_{-+}\bigg).

Using (
13
) we calculate

\displaystyle\partial_{1}(Det)

\displaystyle=\partial_{1}(a_{++})a_{--}+\partial_{1}(a_{--})a_{++}-\partial_{1}(a_{+-})a_{-+}-\partial_{1}(a_{-+})a_{+-}=0.

(3)

By (1)
Det
is an invariant of degree
2p
, and by (2) it is in the kernel by
\partial_{1}
. As it is symmetric, it follows that it is also in the kernel by
\partial_{2}
and
\partial_{3}
, so
Det
is a
p
-th power of a polynomial. The only symmetric polynomials in degree
2p
which are also
p
-th powers are scalar multiples of
\sigma_{2}^{p}
. This scalar depends on
c
. As the coefficients of
v_{+},v_{-}
depend on
c
polynomially, so does
Det
.

∎

In order to analyse the module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
, we will need to show that
v_{+},v_{-}
are (for generic
c
and some special
c
) as independent as they can possibly be, and this will be done by showing that
Det
is nonzero (for generic
c
and some special
c
). This will include analysing the polynomial
f
in
c
and its zeroes. More on that in the next section, but for now let us prove the following Lemma.

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
f(c)\in\Bbbk[c]
be the polynomial from Proposition
6.12
.

(1)

It satisfies
f(c)=\frac{4}{3}\left(3\beta_{0}-\delta_{0}\right)\left(3\beta_{0}+\delta_{0}\right)
;

(2)

All coefficients
\beta_{j}
are even and all
\delta_{j}
are odd polynomials in
\Bbbk[c]
.

(3)

If
p=6k+1
then
\deg\beta_{j}=2k-2j
,
\deg\delta_{j}=2k-2j-1
, and
\deg f=\frac{p-1}{3}
.

(4)

If
p=6k+5
then
\deg\beta_{j}=2k-2j+2
,
\deg\delta_{j}=2k-2j+1
, and
\deg f=\frac{p+1}{3}
.

(5)

The polynomial
f(c)
is not identically zero.

Proof.

(1)

Proposition
6.12
(3) showed that
Det=a_{++}a_{--}-a_{+-}a_{-+}
is a scalar multiple of
\sigma_{2}^{p}
, so we now calculate the scalar
f(c)
by considering only the parts of
a_{++}
,
a_{--}
,
a_{+-}
and
a_{-+}
which will contribute to a power of
\sigma_{2}
. More specifically, any term divisible by
\sigma_{3}
will not contribute to
Det
. Disregarding all such terms, we can write:

\displaystyle a_{++}

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\cdot(-b_{+})+\sigma_{3}\cdot(\ldots)

\displaystyle a_{+-}

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\cdot 3b_{-}+\delta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}}\cdot q+\sigma_{3}\cdot(\ldots)

\displaystyle a_{-+}

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\cdot b_{-}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}}\cdot q+\sigma_{3}\cdot(\ldots)

\displaystyle a_{--}

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\cdot b_{+}+\sigma_{3}\cdot(\ldots).

From here we then compute:

\displaystyle Det

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-b_{+})\beta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}b_{+}-\left(\beta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}3b_{-}+\delta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}}q\right)\left(\beta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}b_{-}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{0}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}}q\right)+\sigma_{3}\cdot(\ldots)

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{p-1}(-b_{+}^{2}-3b_{-}^{2})+\frac{1}{3}\delta_{0}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{p-3}q^{2}+\sigma_{3}\cdot(\ldots)

\displaystyle=12\beta_{0}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{p}+\frac{1}{3}\delta_{0}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{p-3}(-27\sigma_{3}^{2}-4\sigma_{2}^{3})+\sigma_{3}\cdot(\ldots)

\displaystyle=\left(12\beta_{0}^{2}-\frac{4}{3}\delta_{0}^{2}\right)\sigma_{2}^{p}+\sigma_{3}\cdot(\ldots).

Comparing this with
Det=f(c)\sigma_{2}^{p}
we get that

f(c)=12\beta_{0}^{2}-\frac{4}{3}\delta_{0}^{2}=\frac{4}{3}\left(3\beta_{0}-\delta_{0}\right)\left(3\beta_{0}+\delta_{0}\right).

(2)

Clear from the recursion in Lemma
6.5
.

(3)

Clear from the recursion in Lemma
6.5
.

(4)

It is enough to see that the polynomial
f(c)
is nonzero at a specific point, so set
c=0
. Then
\delta_{j}=0
for all
j
and
f(0)=12\beta_{0}^{2}
, which is nonzero by the recursion in Lemma
6.5
.

∎

We will now use this determinant to analyse the modules
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm}\right>
and
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>
.

Lemma 6.14
.

Let
c
be generic.

(1)

The submodule
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
is isomorphic to
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-p]
.

(2)

The vectors
\sigma_{2}^{p}\otimes b_{+},\sigma_{2}^{p}\otimes b_{-}
are in
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
.

(3)

The vectors
\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{+}
and
\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{-}
are not in
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
.

(4)

The module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>
has the character

\chi_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>}(z)=\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\cdot[\mathrm{Stand}]\cdot(1-z^{p})(1-z^{3p})

and the Hilbert series

h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>}(z)=2\frac{(1-z^{p})(1-z^{3p})}{(1-z)^{2}}.

(5)

The module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>
is irreducible and thus equal to
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
.

Proof.

(1)

The
S_{3}
-map
\varphi:\mathrm{Stand}\to M_{1,c}^{p}(\mathrm{Stand})
given by
\varphi(b_{\pm})=v_{\pm}
induces a map of
H_{1,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
modules
\varphi:M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-p]\to M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
whose image is
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
. Let us show its kernel is zero.

Assume that
A,B\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
are homogeneous and that
\varphi(A\otimes b_{+}+B\otimes b_{-})=0
. This can be written as

\displaystyle A\cdot a_{++}+B\cdot a_{-+}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle A\cdot a_{+-}+B\cdot a_{--}

\displaystyle=0.

Considering this as a linear system of equations, we note that its determinant is, by Theorem
6.12
and Lemma
6.13
, equal to
f(c)\sigma_{2}^{p}
, which is nonzero for generic
c
, and thus for generic
c
the only solution of this system is
A=B=0
.

(2)

Checking if
\sigma_{2}^{p}\otimes b_{+}
is in the
H_{t,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
submodule
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
is equivalent to trying to find
A,B\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
such that

Av_{+}+Bv_{-}=\sigma_{2}^{p}\otimes b_{+}.

This can be rewritten as the system

\displaystyle A\cdot a_{++}+B\cdot a_{-+}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle A\cdot a_{+-}+B\cdot a_{--}

\displaystyle=0,

which, for generic
c
where
f(c)\neq 0
, has a unique solution

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{2}^{p}\cdot a_{--}}{Det}=\frac{1}{f(c)}\cdot a_{--}

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\frac{-\sigma_{2}^{p}\cdot a_{+-}}{Det}=-\frac{1}{f(c)}\cdot a_{+-}.

This shows that whenever
f(c)\neq 0
,
\sigma_{2}^{p}\otimes b_{+}
is in
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
. The vector
\sigma_{2}^{p}\otimes b_{-}
can be obtained from
\sigma_{2}^{p}\otimes b_{+}
by the
S_{3}
action, so the claim follows.

(3)

Similarly, checking if the vector
\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{+}
is in
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
is equivalent to checking if there arehomogeneous
A,B\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
such that

Av_{+}+Bv_{-}=\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{+},

which is a system with a unique rational solution

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{3}^{p}\cdot a_{--}}{f(c)\sigma_{2}^{p}}

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\frac{-\sigma_{3}^{p}\cdot a_{+-}}{f(c)\sigma_{2}^{p}}.

These
A,B
are rational function in
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
and not elements of
S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
, and they are the only solutions when
f(c)\neq 0
. So, we conclude that
\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}
do not lie in
\left<v_{\pm}\right>
.

(4)

The character
\chi_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>}(z)
equals

\displaystyle\chi_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)-\chi_{\left<v_{\pm}\right>}(z)-\chi_{\left<\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes\pm\right>}(z)+\chi_{\left<v_{\pm}\right>\cap\left<\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>}(z).

By (1),
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>\cong M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-p]
and
\left<\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{+},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{-}\right>\cong M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-3p]
, so

\chi_{\left<v_{\pm}\right>}(z)=\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}[\mathrm{Stand}]\cdot z^{p},\,\,\chi_{\left<\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>}(z)=\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}[\mathrm{Stand}]\cdot z^{3p},

and the task is to describe
\left<v_{\pm}\right>\cap\left<\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>
.

Let
v\in\left<v_{\pm}\right>\cap\left<\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>
be arbitrary. There exist
A,B,C,D\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
such that

(14)

v=Av_{+}+Bv_{-}=C\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{+}+D\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{-}.

As before, we get

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\frac{C\cdot a_{--}+D\cdot a_{-+}}{f(c)\sigma_{2}^{p}}\sigma_{3}^{p}

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\frac{-C\cdot a_{+-}+D\cdot a_{++}}{f(c)\sigma_{2}^{p}}\sigma_{3}^{p}.

Given that
\sigma_{2}
and
\sigma_{3}
are algebraically independent so their powers are coprime, it follows that
\sigma_{2}^{p}
needs to divide
C\cdot a_{--}+D\cdot a_{-+}
and
-C\cdot a_{+-}+D\cdot a_{++}
, and
\sigma_{3}^{p}
needs to divide both
A
and
B
. In other words, there are
A^{\prime},B^{\prime}\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
such that

A=A^{\prime}\cdot\sigma_{3}^{p},\quad B=B^{\prime}\cdot\sigma_{3}^{p}.

Rewriting equation (
14
) we get

v=A^{\prime}\sigma_{3}^{p}v_{+}+B^{\prime}\sigma_{3}^{p}v_{-}=C\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{+}+D\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{-}.

For any choice of
A^{\prime},B^{\prime}\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
there are unique
C,D\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
satisfying this, given by

C=A^{\prime}a_{++}+B^{\prime}a_{-+},\quad D=A^{\prime}a_{+-}+B^{\prime}a_{--}.

This lets us conclude that

\left<v_{\pm}\right>\cap\left<\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>=\left<\sigma_{3}^{p}v_{\pm}\right>,\quad\chi_{\left<\sigma_{3}^{p}v_{\pm}\right>}(z)=\chi_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)\cdot z^{4p},

and the character of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>
follows.

(5)

The module
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
is a quotient of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>
of dimension
6p^{2}
. The above shows that

\dim M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>(z)=h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{\pm},\sigma_{3}^{p}\otimes b_{\pm}\right>}(1)=6p^{2},

so they are equal.

∎

7.
Irreducible representations of
H_{t,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
in characteristic
p>3
for special
c

We now turn our attention to special values of the parameter
c
, which are not covered by Theorem
6.1
. The value of the characteristic remains
p>3
. When
t=0
the only special value of
c
is
c=0
, and when
t=1
the special values of
c
are
0,1,2\ldots,p-1
. We will sometimes consider these
c
as elements of
\mathbb{Z}
to allow us to write inequalities such as
0<c<p/6
, and at other times (when they are coefficients in the algebra), we consider them as elements of
\mathbb{F}_{p}\subseteq\Bbbk
.

The aim of this section is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 7.1
.

The characters of the irreducible representations
L_{t,c}(\tau)
of the rational Cherednik algebra
H_{t,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
over an algebraically closed field of characteristic
p>3
, for all special
c
,
t=0,1
, and any
\tau
, are given by the following tables.

Characters:

p>3

\mathrm{Triv}

t=0
,
c=0

[\mathrm{Triv}]

t=1
,
c=0

\chi_{S^{(p)}\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)

t=1,0<c<p/3

\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\cdot(1-z^{3c+p}[\mathrm{Stand}]+z^{2(3c+p)}[\mathrm{Sign}])

[Lian]

t=1,p/3<c<p/2

\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\cdot(1-z^{3c-p}[\mathrm{Stand}]+z^{2(3c-p)}[\mathrm{Sign}])

[Lian]

t=1,p/2<c<2p/3

\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)(1-z^{6c-3p}[\mathrm{Sign}])(1-z^{p})

t=1,2p/3<c<p

\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\cdot(1-z^{3c-2p}[\mathrm{Stand}]+z^{2(3c-2p)}[\mathrm{Sign}])

[Lian]

p>3

\mathrm{Sign}

t=0
,
c=0

[\mathrm{Sign}]

t=1
,
c=0

\chi_{S^{(p)}\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)[\mathrm{Sign}]

t=1,0<c<p/3

\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\cdot([\mathrm{Sign}]-z^{p-3c}[\mathrm{Stand}]+z^{2(p-3c)}[\mathrm{Triv}])

t=1,p/3<c<p/2

\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)([\mathrm{Sign}]-z^{3p-6c}[\mathrm{Triv}])(1-z^{p})

t=1,p/2<c<2p/3

t=1,2p/3<c<p

\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\cdot([\mathrm{Sign}]-z^{4p-3c}[\mathrm{Stand}]+z^{2(4p-3c)}[\mathrm{Triv}])

p>3

\mathrm{Stand}

t=0
,

(Conjecture)

\left.-z^{3c}[\mathrm{Sign}]-z^{4p-3c}[\mathrm{Triv}]-z^{2p+3c}[\mathrm{Sign}]+z^{4p}[\mathrm{Stand}]\right)

(Conjecture)

t=1,

\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\cdot\left([\mathrm{Stand}]-[\mathrm{Sign}]z^{3c-2p}-\right.

Hilbert polynomials:

p>3

,

,

\left(\frac{1-z^{p}}{1-z}\right)^{2}

\left(\frac{1-z^{p-3c}}{1-z}\right)^{2}

\frac{1-z^{p-3c}-2z^{p}-z^{p+3c}+2z^{2p}}{(1-z)^{2}}

[

, Thm 3.3]

\left(\frac{1-z^{3c-p}}{1-z}\right)^{2}

\frac{1-z^{-p+3c}-z^{3p-3c}-z^{p+3c}-z^{5p-3c}+2z^{4p}}{(1-z)^{2}}

, Thm 3.3]
