\frac{(1-z^{6c-3p})(1-z^{p})}{(1-z)^{2}}

\left(\frac{1-z^{2p-3c}}{1-z}\right)^{2}

\frac{1-z^{2p-3c}-z^{3c}-z^{4p-3c}-z^{2p+3c}+2z^{4p}}{(1-z)^{2}}

(Conjecture)

2p/3<c<p

\left(\frac{1-z^{3c-2p}}{1-z}\right)^{2}

\left(\frac{1-z^{4p-3c}}{1-z}\right)^{2}

\frac{1-z^{3c-2p}-2z^{p}-z^{4p-3c}+2z^{2p}}{(1-z)^{2}}

[
Lia12
, Thm 3.3]

In all cases, the singular vectors are known and given in the proofs.

To write these characters explicitly, recall that, from Corollaries
2.7
and
2.8
,

\displaystyle\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-z^{2})(1-z^{3})}\left([\mathrm{Triv}]+(z+z^{2})[\mathrm{Stand}]+z^{3}[\mathrm{Sign}]\right),

\displaystyle\chi_{S^{(p)}\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)

\displaystyle=\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\cdot(1-z^{p}[\mathrm{Stand}]+z^{2p}[\mathrm{Sign}]).

Proof.

For all
\tau
and
t
, the case
c=0
is standard, and explained in Lemma
2.17
for
t=0
and Lemmas
2.18
for
t=1
.

For
\tau=\mathrm{Triv}
, the remaining computations for
t=1
,
c\neq 0\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
fall into several cases depending on where
c
lies in the set
0,1,\ldots,p-1
. The paper
[
Lia12
]
deals with all these intervals except one,
p/2<c<2p/3
, where they give conjectured degrees of the generators. We explain the work of
[
Lia12
]
in Section
7.1
and work out the remaining case
p/2<c<2p/3
in Theorem
7.4
(giving the generators of the maximal proper graded submodule, the Hilbert polynomial, the character, and showing this is a complete intersection).

For
\tau=\mathrm{Sign}
, the character formulas follow from the character formulas for
\mathrm{Triv}
by Lemma
2.15
.

For
\tau=\mathrm{Stand}

t=1
and
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, the case
0<c<p/3
is done with all the proofs in Section
7.3
, the case
p/3<c<p/2
is done conjecturally and with no proofs in Section
7.4
, and the cases
p/2<c<p
follow from them using Lemma
2.15
and the fact that
\mathrm{Stand}\otimes\mathrm{Sign}\cong\mathrm{Stand}
.

∎

7.1.
Results of Lian
[
Lia12
]

The paper
[
Lia12
]
deals with some special values of
c
for
\tau=\mathrm{Triv}
. Here we restate their Theorem 3.3. in our conventions.

Proposition 7.2

(
[
Lia12
]
Theorem 3.3.)
.

Let
\Bbbk
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
p>3
,
t=1
and
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and consider the rational Cherednik algebra
H_{1,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
. The degrees of the generators of the maximal proper graded submodule of the Verma module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
are as follows:

(1)

0<c<p/3
: two generators of degree
3c+p
,

(2)

p/3<c<p/2
: two generators of degree
3c-p
,

(3)

2p/3<c<p
: two generators of degree
3c-2p
.

These generators are given explicitly in the proof of
[
Lia12
]
Theorem 3.3. In each case the irreducible quotient
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
is a complete intersection, so letting
a\in\mathbb{N}
be the degree of the generators, its character and Hilbert polynomial are

\displaystyle\chi_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})}(z)

\displaystyle=\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\cdot(1-z^{a}[\mathrm{Stand}]+z^{2a}[\mathrm{Sign}]),

\displaystyle h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})}(z)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1-z^{a}}{1-z}\right)^{2}.

Remark 7.3
.

Note some differences between this proposition and the theorem as stated in
[
Lia12
]
. Firstly,
[
Lia12
]
works with the reflection representation
V
and we work with
\mathfrak{h}
; the dictionary for translating results from one setting to the other is given in Proposition
2.2
and consequently we list one generator fewer (
\sigma_{1}^{p}
, which is
0
in our setting). Secondly, there is a typo in the statement of Case (3) of
[
Lia12
]
; the correct degrees are listed here and the proof in
[
Lia12
]
is correct. Thirdly, we calculate the characters, which is straightforward from the information
[
Lia12
]
provide. Fourthly, the results of
[
Lia12
]
are missing for
p/2<c<2p/3
, where they conjecturally give the degrees of the generators. We address this in detail in Section
7.2
, calculating the generators,the character of the quotient, and showing the quotient is irreducible. Finally, we want to point out that the results of
[
Lia12
]
in the cases
0<c<p/2
and
2p/3<c<p
lack three steps in the proofs: 1) a minor step showing, (in their notation) that
D_{2}(G_{1})=0
, which is completely straightforward; 2) the proof that the quotient is a complete intersection and thus its Hilbert polynomial is as stated, which they explicitly assume in Theorem 3.3.; and 3) the proof that the quotient is irreducible, which relies on the yet unpublished results by Roman Bezrukavnikov and Andrei Okounkov. We do not do this here, but note that these results all appear correct and can be verified using similar methods to our work in the
p/2<c<2p/3
case.

7.2.
Irreducible representation
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})

The aim of this section is to describe
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
in the non-modular case
p>3
for the values of
c
that
[
Lia12
]
does not cover, namely
p/2<c<2p/3
. We fix those values of the parameter throughout the section. The main result is:

Theorem 7.4
.

The irreducible representation
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
of the rational Cherednik algebra
H_{1,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
over an algebraically closed field of characteristic
p>3
for
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
p/2<c<2p/3
, is the quotient of the Verma module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
by the submodule generated by the vectors

\displaystyle v_{6c-3p}

\displaystyle=q^{2c-p}=(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}q

\displaystyle v_{p}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{2}^{2p-3c}\sigma_{3}\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}{c-\frac{p+1}{2}\choose j}\frac{1}{2j+1}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-j}(-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{j}.

This quotient is a complete intersection, and its character and Hilbert polynomial are

\displaystyle\chi_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})}(z)

\displaystyle=\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)(1-z^{6c-3p}[\mathrm{Sign}]-z^{p}[\mathrm{Triv}]+z^{6c-2p}[\mathrm{Sign}])

\displaystyle h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-z^{6c-3p})(1-z^{p})}{(1-z)^{2}}.

Proof.

Firstly, let us use the Casimir element
\Omega
. If
M
is a
H_{t,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
module equal to
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
or one of its quotients, and
\tau\subseteq M^{l}
is an irreducible
S_{3}
-subrepresentation consisting of singular vectors, then by Lemma
2.1

\Omega
acts on
\tau
by the following scalar:

(15)

\Omega|_{\mathrm{Triv}}=0\cdot\mathrm{id},\quad\Omega|_{\mathrm{Sign}}=6c\cdot\mathrm{id},\quad\Omega|_{\mathrm{Stand}}=3c\cdot\mathrm{id}.

At the same time, because
\tau\subseteq M^{l}
,
\Omega
acts on
\tau
by
l\cdot\mathrm{id}
.
As a consequence, singular vectors of type
\mathrm{Triv}
in
M
can only appear in degrees of the form
kp
, singular vectors of type
\mathrm{Sign}
in degrees of the form
6c+kp
, and singular vectors of type
\mathrm{Stand}
in degrees of the form
3c+kp
.

Secondly, note that for
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
with
\frac{p}{2}<c<\frac{2p}{3}
we have

(16)

0<6c-3p<3c-p<p,

and these are the only integers of the form
kp,3c+kp
and
6c+kp
with
k\in\mathbb{Z}
in the interval
[0,p]
.

These two facts together now spell out a strategy for describing
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
: starting from the lowest degrees, we test for singular vectors. The first fact tells us in which degrees can the singular vectors appear, and in which isotypic component. Then we use the basis from Theorem
2.4
to reduce the size of the space where we are looking for the singular vectors, by only looking at specific isotypic components in specific degrees. The second fact tells us in which order to look at these graded pieces. This order is important because as soon as we take a quotient by some submodule in some degree, all calculations in higher degrees need to be done in the quotient module and no longer in the Verma module. This is because there could be singular vectors in the quotient of the Verma module which do not lift to a singular vector in the Verma module. (See Lemma
7.8
for an example of this.)

By equations (
15
) and (
16
), the first space to consider is the
\mathrm{Sign}
isotypic component in degree
6c-3p
. In Lemma
7.5
we show that

v_{6c-3p}=q^{2c-p}=(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}q

is a singular vector in degree
6c-3p
, generating a submodule of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
isomorphic to
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(6c-3p)]
. In Lemma
7.6
we show there are no other singular vectors in degree
6c-3p
.

Next, we take the quotient
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>
and continue examining the degrees as dictated by equations (
15
) and (
16
). In Lemma
7.7
we show that there are no singular vectors in degree
3c-p
of the module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>
. In Lemma
7.8
we show that there is a one dimensional space of singular vectors in degree
p
of the module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>
, in the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component and spanned by

v_{p}=\sigma_{2}^{2p-3c}\sigma_{3}\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}{c-\frac{p+1}{2}\choose j}\frac{1}{2j+1}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-j}(-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{j}.

We note that
v_{p}
is only singular in the quotient
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
, and is not an image of a singular vector in
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
.

In Lemma
7.11

we show that
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<v_{6c-3p},v_{p}\right>
is a complete intersection and calculate its character and Hilbert series. Its Hilbert series turns out to be

h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})}(z)=\frac{(1-z^{6c-3p})(1-z^{p})}{(1-z)^{2}}.

By equations (
15
) and (
16
), the next degree to consider and search for singular vectors is
6c-2p
. However, the above Hilbert series shows that the maximal degree of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<v_{6c-3p},v_{p}\right>
is
6c-3p-1+p-1=6c-2p-2<6c-2p
. This means the quotient
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<v_{6c-3p},v_{p}\right>
has no singular vectors, and is thus irreducible and equal to
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
.

∎

We now proceed with the details of the above proof, listed as a sequence of lemmas. We keep the assumptions on the parameters
t,c,p
listed at the start of Section
7.2
.

Lemma 7.5
.

The vector

v_{6c-3p}=q^{2c-p}=(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}q

is singular in
M_{1,c}^{6c-3p}(\mathrm{Triv})
, and generates a subrepresentation
\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>
isomorphic to
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(6c-3p)]
.

Proof.

The vector
v_{6c-3p}
spans a copy of
\mathrm{Sign}
inside
M_{1,c}^{6c-3p}(\mathrm{Triv})
, so by Lemma
2.20
to check it is singular it is enough to show
D_{y_{1}}(v_{6c-3p})=0
. We check this directly, using computations from Section
5
and the fact that
q^{2c-p-1}
is a symmetric polynomial.

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(q^{2c-p})

\displaystyle=(2c-p)q^{2c-p-1}\partial_{y_{1}}(q)-cq^{2c-p-1}\frac{id-(12)}{x_{1}-x_{2}}(q)-cq^{2c-p-1}\frac{id-(13)}{x_{1}-x_{3}}(q)

\displaystyle=cq^{2c-p-1}\left(2\cdot\frac{-1}{4}(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})+2\cdot\frac{1}{4}(2b_{+}b_{-})+2\sigma_{2}+\frac{1}{3}(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})-\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\left.-2\sigma_{2}+\frac{1}{6}(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})-\frac{1}{2}\cdot 2b_{+}b_{-}\right)=0.

This shows
v_{6c-3p}
is singular, so it induces a map

\varphi:M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(6c-3p)]\to M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv}).

Seen as a map
S\mathfrak{h}^{*}[-(6c-3p)]\cong M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(6c-3p)]\to M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})\cong S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
this is multiplication by
q^{2c-p}
which is injective. So,
\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>\cong M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(6c-3p)]
.
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

The only singular vectors in
M_{1,c}^{6c-3p}(\mathrm{Triv})
are multiples of
v_{6c-3p}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
2.4
we are looking for all singular vectors of the form

v=\sum_{i=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\alpha_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i}\sigma_{3}^{2c-p-1-2i}\cdot q.

The condition
D_{y_{1}}(v)=0
yields, using Section
5
, the system

\alpha_{i+1}=\frac{2^{2}}{3^{3}}\frac{(c-\frac{p+1}{2}-i)}{i+1}\alpha_{i},\quad\quad 0\leq i\leq c-\frac{p+1}{2},

which has a unique (up to overalll scaling) solution

\alpha_{i}=(-1)^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}4^{i}\cdot 27^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-i}\cdot{{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\choose i},\quad 0\leq i\leq c-\frac{p+1}{2},

leading to

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=(-1)^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}{{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\choose i}4^{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i}\cdot 27^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-i}\sigma_{3}^{2(c-\frac{p+1}{2}-i)}\cdot q

\displaystyle=(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\cdot q=q^{2c-p}=v_{6c-3p}.

∎

Lemma 7.7
.

There are no singular vectors in degree
3c-p
of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>
.

Proof.

It is straightforward to show that there are no singular vectors in degree
3c-p
of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
, using a similar computation to the above and the basis

(17)

\left\{\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}b_{+}\mid 2a+3b+1=3c-p\right\}\cup\left\{\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\mid 2a+3b+2=3c-p\right\},

of the
S_{2}
invariant part of the
\mathrm{Stand}
isotypic component of
M_{1,c}^{3c-p}(\mathrm{Triv})
. However, this lemma claims more - that there are no singular vectors modulo
v_{6c-3p}=q^{2c-p}
. The basis (
17
) is not well suited to taking this quotient and the calculations are more involved, so we first change basis to the one in which taking this quotient becomes very easy. We then prove the lemma by direct computation. We distinguish two cases, depending on the remainder of
p
modulo
3
.

Case 1.
p\equiv 2\pmod{3}

The new basis of the
S_{2}
invariant part of the
\mathrm{Stand}
isotypic component of
M_{1,c}^{3c-p}(\mathrm{Triv})
we propose to use in this case is

(18)

\left\{q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}b_{+}\mid 0\leq i\leq\frac{c}{2}-\frac{p+1}{6}\right\}\cup\left\{q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}b_{-}\mid 0\leq i\leq\frac{c}{2}-\frac{p+1}{6}-\frac{1}{2}\right\}.

This set is indeed a basis as it lies in the
S_{2}
invariant part of the
\mathrm{Stand}
isotypic component of
M_{1,c}^{3c-p}(\mathrm{Triv})
, is linearly independent and has the correct number of elements.

After taking the quotient by
\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
the set (
18
) is reduced to

(19)

\left\{q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}b_{+}\mid 0\leq i\leq c-\frac{p+1}{2}\right\}\,\cup\,\left\{q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}b_{-}\mid 0\leq i\leq c-\frac{p+1}{2}-1\right\}.

This set spans the
S_{2}
invariant part of the
\mathrm{Stand}
isotypic component of the module
M_{1,c}^{3c-p}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
and contains
2c-p
elements. On the other hand, the multiplicity of
\mathrm{Stand}
in

M_{1,c}^{3c-p}(\mathrm{Triv})
is
\frac{3c-p+2}{3}
, the multiplicity of
\mathrm{Stand}
in
M_{1,c}^{3c-p}(\mathrm{Sign})[6c-3p]
is
\frac{2p-3c+2}{3}
, so the multiplicity of
\mathrm{Stand}
in
M_{1,c}^{3c-p}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
is

\frac{3c-p+2}{3}-\frac{2p-3c+2}{3}=2c-p.

This shows that the set (
19
) is a basis. We calculate the Dunkl operators in this basis:

(20)

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}\cdot b_{+})=

\displaystyle\quad=(1-\frac{3c+p+1}{2})q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{1}{2}(c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i)q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}+(-27i)q^{2i-1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i+1}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(-3i)q^{2i-1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}\sigma_{2}b_{+}+\frac{1}{6}(c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i)q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}\sigma_{2}b_{+}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(-3i)q^{2i-1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}\sigma_{2}b_{-}-\frac{1}{2}(c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i)q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}\sigma_{2}b_{-},

(21)

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}\cdot b_{-})=

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{-1}{6}(c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1)q^{2i+2}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-2}+\frac{9}{2}(2i+1-2c)q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(1+\frac{3}{2}(c-\frac{p+1}{3}))q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(\frac{-1}{2}(2i+1)+c)q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}\sigma_{2}b_{+}-\frac{1}{6}(c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1)q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-2}\sigma_{2}b_{+}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(\frac{3}{2}(2i+1)-3c)q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}\sigma_{2}b_{-}-\frac{1}{6}(c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1)q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-2}\sigma_{2}b_{-}.

Assume that

w=\sum_{i=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\alpha_{i}q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}\cdot b_{+}+\sum_{i=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-1}\beta_{i}q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}\cdot b_{-}

is a singular vector.
Using (
20
) and (
21
), we get that the
\mathrm{Triv}
component of
D_{y_{1}}(w)
is
0
if and only if

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\alpha_{i}\left(1-\frac{3c+p+1}{2}\right)q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}+

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-1}\beta_{i}\frac{-1}{6}(c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1)q^{2i+2}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-2}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-1}\beta_{i}\frac{9}{2}(2i+1-2c)q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}=0.

This leads to

(22)

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}\cdot\left(1-\frac{3c+p+1}{2}\right)+\beta_{0}\cdot\frac{9}{2}(1-2c)

\displaystyle=0,

(23)

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}\cdot\left(1-\frac{3c+p+1}{2}\right)+\beta_{i}\cdot\frac{9}{2}(2i+1-2c)+\beta_{i-1}\cdot\frac{1}{6}(2i-1-c+\frac{p+1}{3})

\displaystyle=0,

(24)

\displaystyle\alpha_{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\cdot\left(1-\frac{3c+p+1}{2}\right)+\beta_{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-1}\cdot\frac{1}{6}(c-\frac{2(p+1)}{3}-1)

\displaystyle=0.

Similarly, (
20
) and (
21
) imply that the
\mathrm{Sign}
component of
D_{y_{1}}(w)
is
0
if and only if

(25)

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}\cdot\left(c-\frac{p+1}{3}\right)+\beta_{0}\cdot\left(1+\frac{3}{2}(c-\frac{p+1}{2})\right)+\alpha_{1}\cdot(-27)

\displaystyle=0,

(26)

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}\cdot\left(c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i\right)+\beta_{i}\cdot\left(1+\frac{3}{2}(c-\frac{p+1}{2})\right)+\alpha_{i+1}\cdot\left(-27(i+1)\right)

\displaystyle=0,

(27)

\displaystyle\alpha_{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\cdot\left(-c+\frac{2(p+1)}{3}\right)

\displaystyle=0

The system of equations (
22
)-(
27
) can now be shown to have no nonzero solutions by using (
27
) to deduce
\alpha_{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}=0
,
(
24
) to deduce
\beta_{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-1}=0
,
then alternating (
23
) and (
26
) to show that
\beta_{i-1}=0
and
\alpha_{i}=0
for all
i=1,\ldots,c-\frac{p+1}{2}-1
and finally using either (
22
) or (
25
) to show that
\alpha_{0}=0
. The only constants we divide by in this calculation are
c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i
and
c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i+1
, which are never equal to
0
for these
i
and
\frac{p}{2}<c<\frac{2p}{3}
. This shows that the only solution to the equation
D_{y_{1}}(w)=0
in the
S_{2}
invariant part of the
\mathrm{Stand}
isotypic component of
M_{1,c}^{3c-p}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
is
w=0
, proving the claim of the Lemma in Case 1.

Case 2.
p\equiv 1\pmod{3}
Similar. The basis of the
S_{2}
-invariant part of the
\mathrm{Stand}
isotypic component of
M_{1,c}^{3c-p}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
is

\left\{q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+2}{3}-2i}\cdot(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\,,\,q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+2}{3}-2i-1}\cdot 2b_{+}b_{-}\mid 0\leq i<c-\frac{p+1}{2}\right\}.

We calculate the Dunkl operators in this basis to be:

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}\cdot(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2}))=

\displaystyle\quad=2(c-\frac{p+2}{3}-2i)q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+2}{3}-2i-1}\sigma_{2}^{2}+(-9i)q^{2i-1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i}\sigma_{2}^{2}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\textrm{something in the Stand component of }M_{1,c}^{3c-p-1}(\mathrm{Triv})

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-1}\cdot 2b_{+}b_{-}))=

\displaystyle\quad=6(2i+1-2c)q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+2}{3}-2i-1}\sigma_{2}^{2}+2(c-\frac{p+2}{3}-2i-1)q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2i-2}\sigma_{2}^{2}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\textrm{something in the Stand component of }M_{1,c}^{3c-p-1}(\mathrm{Triv}).

Requiring that a vector

w=\sum_{i=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\alpha_{i}q^{2i}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+2}{3}-2i}\cdot(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})+\sum_{i=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-1}\beta_{i}q^{2i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-\frac{p+2}{3}-2i-1}\cdot 2b_{+}b_{-}

satisfies
D_{y_{1}}(w)=0
leads to to a system of equations for
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}
.
By asking that the
\mathrm{Triv}
component of
D_{y_{1}}(w)
is
0
we get

\displaystyle\alpha_{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}\cdot 2(c-\frac{p+2}{3}-2i)+\beta_{i}\cdot 6(2i+1-2c)

\displaystyle=0,\qquad i=0,\ldots,c-\frac{p+1}{2}-1

and by asking that the
\mathrm{Sign}
component of
D_{y_{1}}(w)
is
0
we get

\displaystyle\alpha_{i+1}\cdot(-9)(i+1)+\beta_{i}\cdot 2(c-\frac{p+2}{3}-2i-1)

\displaystyle=0,\qquad i=0,\ldots,c-\frac{p+1}{2}.

This system has a unique solution
\alpha_{i}=0,\beta_{i}=0
for all
i
, so
w=0
. This proves the Lemma in case 2.
∎

Lemma 7.8
.

There is a one dimensional space of singular vectors in degree
p
of the module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
, in the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component and spanned by

v_{p}=\sigma_{2}^{2p-3c}\sigma_{3}\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}{c-\frac{p+1}{2}\choose j}\frac{1}{2j+1}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-j}(-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{j}.

Note that
v_{p}
does not lift to a singular vector in
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
.

Proof.

We will be calculating modulo

q^{2c-p}=(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}q.

The basis we are working with in
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component of
M_{1,c}^{p}(\mathrm{Triv})
is

\left\{\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\,\mid\,2a+3b=p,0\leq a,b\right\}.

In
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
these elements are not linearly independent, as they satisfy

\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}q^{2}=\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}+1}=0,

for all
a,b
such that
2a+3b+6(c-\frac{p+1}{2}+1)=p.
In particular, in this quotient we can express any power of
\sigma_{3}^{b}
with
b\geq 2c-p+1
in terms of polynomials of lower degree in
\sigma_{3}
. The basis of the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})^{p}/\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
is thus

\displaystyle\left\{\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\,\mid\,2a+3b=p,0\leq a,0\leq b<2c-p+1\right\}=\left\{\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}\,\mid\,0\leq j\leq c-\frac{p+1}{2}\right\}.

Write an arbitrary vector
v
in the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component of
M_{1,c}^{p}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
as

v=\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\alpha_{j}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}

for some
\alpha_{j}\in\Bbbk
(depending on
c
), and let us check when
v
is singular. We have

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b})

\displaystyle=\frac{-a}{6}\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b}(b_{+}+3b_{-})+\frac{b}{36}\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})+3\cdot 2b_{+}b_{-}),

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(v)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\alpha_{j}\left(\frac{-(\frac{p-3}{2}-3j)}{6}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j-1}\sigma_{3}^{2j+1}(b_{+}+3b_{-})+\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\frac{2j+1}{36}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j}\sigma_{3}^{2j}((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})+3\cdot 2b_{+}b_{-})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\alpha_{j}\frac{2j+1}{12}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j-1}\sigma_{3}^{2j}\left(3\sigma_{3}b_{+}+\frac{1}{3}\sigma_{2}(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\alpha_{j}\frac{2j+1}{12}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j-1}\sigma_{3}^{2j}\left(-9\sigma_{3}b_{+}-\sigma_{2}\cdot 2b_{+}b_{-}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\alpha_{j}\frac{2j+1}{12}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j-1}\sigma_{3}^{2j}qb_{-}-\alpha_{j}\frac{2j+1}{12}\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}-3j-1}\sigma_{3}^{2j}qb_{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sigma_{2}^{2p-3c}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\alpha_{j}(2j+1)\sigma_{2}^{3(c-\frac{p+1}{2}-j)}\sigma_{3}^{2j}\right)q\cdot(b_{-}-b_{+}).

Recall that we are working in the quotient by
q^{2c-p}
. The above vector
D_{y_{1}}(v)
is a multiple of

q^{2c-p}=(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}q=\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}{c-\frac{p+1}{2}\choose j}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-j}(-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{j}

if and only if (up to overalll scaling) we have for all
j

\alpha_{j}(2j+1)={c-\frac{p+1}{2}\choose j}(-4)^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-j}(-27)^{j}.

This proves there is (up to scalars) exactly one singular vector in the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component of
M_{1,c}^{p}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<q^{2c-p}\right>
equal to

v_{p}=\sigma_{2}^{2p-3c}\sigma_{3}\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}{c-\frac{p+1}{2}\choose j}\frac{1}{2j+1}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-j}(-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{j}.

∎

Remark 7.9
.

We can rewrite it as

v_{p}=\sigma_{2}^{2p-3c}\sigma_{3}\sum_{i=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-i}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3})^{i}\frac{\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}(2m-2j)}{\prod_{j=0}^{i}(2m+1-2j)}

though it is not clear if we gain anything by this nor if there is a closed formula for
v_{p}
.

Remark 7.10
.

When
c=\frac{1}{2}=\frac{p+1}{2}
, it lies in the interval considered here as
p/2<\frac{p+1}{2}<2p/3
. For this
c
the above formula says

v_{p}=\sigma_{2}^{\frac{p-3}{2}}\sigma_{3}.

This special case has already been noted in
[
Lia12
]
Remark 3.5.

Lemma 7.11
.

The vectors
v_{6c-3p},v_{p}
form a regular sequence, and
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<v_{6c-3p},v_{p}\right>
is a complete intersection. Its character and Hilbert polynomial are

\displaystyle\chi_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<v_{6c-3p},v_{p}\right>}(z)

\displaystyle=\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)(1-z^{6c-3p}[\mathrm{Sign}]-z^{p}[\mathrm{Triv}]+z^{6c-2p}[\mathrm{Sign}])

\displaystyle h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})/\left<v_{6c-3p},v_{p}\right>}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-z^{6c-3p})(1-z^{p})}{(1-z)^{2}}.

To prove lemma
7.11
we will need two auxiliary facts, which will not be used elsewhere.

Lemma 7.12
.

For any
m\in\left\{1,2,\ldots,p-1\right\}
we have

\sum_{j=0}^{m}{m\choose j}\frac{1}{2j+1}(-1)^{j}\neq 0.

Proof.

Set

f_{m}(z)=\sum_{j=0}^{m}{m\choose j}\frac{1}{2j+1}(-1)^{j}z^{2j+1}.

The claim is then that
f_{m}(1)\neq 0
.

Differentiating formally we get that

f_{m}^{\prime}(z)=\sum_{j=0}^{m}{m\choose j}(-1)^{j}z^{2j}=(1-z^{2})^{m},

so

f_{m}(z)=\int(1-z^{2})^{m}dz,\quad f_{m}(0)=0

is the primitive function of
(1-z^{2})^{m}
with no constant term.

There is a recursive formula for evaluating this integral, given by

\int(1-z^{2})^{m}dz=\frac{z(1-z^{2})^{m}}{2m+1}+\frac{2m}{2m+1}\int(1-z^{2})^{m-1}dz.

As
\frac{z(1-z^{2})^{m}}{2m+1}|_{z=0}=0
, we have

f_{m}(z)=\frac{z(1-z^{2})^{m}}{2m+1}+\frac{2m}{2m+1}f_{m-1}(z),

and so

f_{m}(1)=0+\frac{2m}{2m+1}f_{m-1}(1)

and in particular (using
m<p
and
p\neq 2
) we get
f_{m}(1)\neq 0
if and only if
f_{m-1}(1)\neq 0
. Inductively, it remains to show that
f_{1}(1)\neq 0
, which is straightforward from

f_{1}(z)=\int 1-z^{2}dz=z-\frac{1}{3}z^{3}

and

f_{1}(1)=\frac{2}{3}\neq 0.

∎

Before the next lemma, recall that
q^{2}=-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2}.

Lemma 7.13
.

If
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
A,B\in(S\mathfrak{h}^{*})^{S_{3}}
are symmetric polynomials such that

A(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{k}=Bv_{p},

then there exists a symmetric polynomial
C
such that

A=Cv_{p},\quad B=C(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{k}.

Proof.

This is an statement about symmetric polynomials, which, by the the fundamental theorem of symmetric polynomials form an algebra isomorphic to a polynomial algebra
(S\mathfrak{h}^{*})^{S_{3}}\cong\Bbbk[a,b]
with the isomorphism
\sigma_{2}\mapsto a,\sigma_{3}\mapsto b
. Abusing notation slightly, the problem becomes about
A,B\in\Bbbk[a,b]
such that

A(-4a^{3}-27b^{2})^{k}=Ba^{2p-3c}b\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}{c-\frac{p+1}{2}\choose j}\frac{1}{2j+1}(-4a^{3})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-j}(-27b^{2})^{j}.

The polynomial
-4a^{3}-27b^{2}
is irreducible. It does not divide the polynomial
v_{p}=a^{2p-3c}b\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}{c-\frac{p+1}{2}\choose j}\frac{1}{2j+1}(-4a^{3})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-j}(-27b^{2})^{j}
; to see that, plug in the values
a=-3
and
b=2
to get that

-4(-3)^{3}-27\cdot 2^{2}=0
, while by Lemma
7.12

\displaystyle v_{p}|_{a=-3,b=2}

\displaystyle=(-3)^{2p-3c}\cdot 2\cdot(4\cdot 27)^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}{c-\frac{p+1}{2}\choose j}\frac{1}{2j+1}(-1)^{j}\neq 0.

So,
(-4a^{3}-27b^{2})
divides
B
, and proceeding inductively we get that
(-4a^{3}-27b^{2})^{k}
divides
B
. This means there exists
C\in\Bbbk[a,b]
such that

B=(-4a^{3}-27b^{2})^{k}C.

It now follows that

A=C\cdot a^{2p-3c}b\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}{c-\frac{p+1}{2}\choose j}\frac{1}{2j+1}(-4a^{3})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}-j}(-27b^{2})^{j}.

Replacing
a,b
by
\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}
we get the claim.

∎

Proof of Lemma
7.11
.

We claim that in
S\mathfrak{h}^{*}\cong M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
we have

\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>\cap\left<v_{p}\right>=\left<v_{6c-3p}v_{p}\right>.

This is enough to show that the sequence is regular, as it is equivalent to saying that
v_{p}
is not a zero divisor in
S\mathfrak{h}^{*}/\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>
.

Consider any vector
u\in\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>\cap\left<v_{p}\right>
. As we are in the non-modular case, by using
S_{3}
projection formulas we can assume that
u
is in an isotypic component of type
\mathrm{Triv}
,
\mathrm{Sign}
, or
\mathrm{Stand}
. It is of the form

u=Av_{6c-3p}=Bv_{p}

for some
A,B\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.
Recall that

v_{6c-3p}=q^{2c-p}=(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}q,

that
v_{6c-3p}
is in the
\mathrm{Sign}
isotypic component and that
v_{p}
is in the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component.

Case 1.
If the vector
u
is in the
\mathrm{Sign}
isotypic component then
A
is symmetric and
B
is antisymmetric. Write
B=B^{\prime}\cdot q
for some symmetric polynomial
B^{\prime}
. The problem now becomes finding
A,B^{\prime}\in(S\mathfrak{h}^{*})^{S_{3}}
such that

A(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}=B^{\prime}v_{p}

Using Lemma
7.13
we get that there exists a symmetric polynomial
C
such that

A=Cv_{p},B^{\prime}=C(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}},B=C(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}q=Cq^{2c-p}

and so the vector

u=Cv_{6c-3p}v_{p}
lies in
\left<v_{6c-3p}v_{p}\right>
as claimed.

Case 2.
If the vector
u
is in the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component then
A
is antisymmetric and
B
is symmetric. Write
A=A^{\prime}\cdot q
for some symmetric polynomial
A^{\prime}
, reducing the problem to

A^{\prime}\cdot(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}+1}=Bv_{p}

with
A^{\prime},B\in(S\mathfrak{h}^{*})^{S_{3}}
. Using Lemma
7.13
again we get that there exists a symmetric polynomial
C
such that

A^{\prime}=Cv_{p},A=Cv_{p}q,B=C(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}+1}=Cq^{2c-p+1},

and that
so the vector

u=Cv_{6c-3p}v_{p}q
lies in
\left<v_{6c-3p}v_{p}\right>
as claimed.

Case 3.
If the vector
u
is in the
\mathrm{Stand}
isotypic component, we can assume without loss of generality that it is
S_{2}
invariant. Then
A
lies in the
S_{2}
anti-invariant part of the
\mathrm{Stand}
isotypic component of
S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
,
B
in the
S_{2}
invariant, and using the basis from Theorem
2.4
we can write

A=A_{1}b_{-}+A_{2}\cdot 2b_{+}b_{-},\quad B=B_{1}b_{+}+B_{2}(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})

for some
A_{1},A_{2},B_{1},B_{2}\in(S\mathfrak{h}^{*})^{S_{3}}.
The equation
Aq^{2c-p}=Bv_{p}
becomes

(A_{1}b_{-}q+A_{2}\cdot 2b_{+}b_{-}q)(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}=(B_{1}b_{+}+B_{2}(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2}))v_{p},

which can be rewritten as the system

\displaystyle\left(A_{1}\cdot 3\sigma_{3}+A_{2}\cdot 4\sigma_{2}^{2}\right)(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{1}v_{p}

\displaystyle\left(A_{1}\cdot\frac{1}{3}\sigma_{2}-A_{2}\cdot 3\sigma_{3}\right)(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}

\displaystyle=B_{2}v_{p}.

This is again a problem about symmetric polynomials. We use Lemma
7.13
twice to get that there exist symmetric polynomials
C_{1},C_{2}
such that

A_{1}\cdot 3\sigma_{3}+A_{2}\cdot 4\sigma_{2}^{2}=C_{1}v_{p},\quad B_{1}=C_{1}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}

A_{1}\cdot\frac{1}{3}\sigma_{2}-A_{2}\cdot 3\sigma_{3}=C_{2}v_{p},\quad B_{2}=C_{2}(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})^{c-\frac{p+1}{2}}.

From here we get

\displaystyle A_{1}\cdot(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})

\displaystyle=(-3)(C_{1}\cdot 3\sigma_{3}+C_{2}\cdot 4\sigma_{2}^{2})v_{p}

\displaystyle A_{2}\cdot(-4\sigma_{2}^{3}-27\sigma_{3}^{2})

\displaystyle=(-3)(C_{1}\cdot\frac{1}{3}\sigma_{2}^{3}+C_{2}\cdot 3\sigma_{3})v_{p}.

We now use Lemma
7.13
twice more to get that there exist symmetric polynomials
D_{1},D_{2}
such that

A_{1}=D_{1}v_{p},A_{2}=D_{2}v_{p}.

Finally, it follows that the vector
u=Aq^{2c-p}=Bv_{p}
can be written as

u=Av_{6c-3p}=(D_{1}b_{-}+D_{2}\cdot 2b_{+}b_{-})v_{pc-3p}v_{p},

which proves it in
\left<v_{pc-3p}v_{p}\right>
as claimed.

The computation of the character and the Hilbert series is now standard, using that
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
is a quotient of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})
by
\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>\cong M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(6c-3p)]
and
\left<v_{p}\right>\cong M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})[-p]
, and that
\left<v_{6c-3p}\right>\cap\left<v_{p}\right>=\left<v_{6c-3p}v_{p}\right>\cong M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(6c-2p)]
.
∎

7.3.
Irreducible representation
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
, part 1

The aim of this Section is to prove Theorem
7.15
, which describes
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
for
p>3
and
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
0<c<p/3
. We have only been able to prove Lemma
7.18
, used in the proof of Theorem
7.15
, with the following technical assumption on the parameter
c
. We believe the assumption is satisfied for all
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
with
0<c<p/3
.

Assumption 7.14
.

Let
p>3
and
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
with
0<c<p/3
. Assume that
c
satisfies one of the following:

(1)

Either
0<c<p/6
;

(2)

Or
p\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,
p/6<c<p/3
, and

\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-1}{3}-c)\rfloor}\frac{1}{3^{k}}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{k}(3j-1)(\frac{p-1}{3}-c)^{\underline{2k}}}{k!(c-2)^{\underline{2k}}}\neq 0;

(3)

Or
p\equiv 2\pmod{3}
,
c=\frac{p+1}{6}
;

(4)

Or
p\equiv 2\pmod{3}
,
(p+1)/6<c<p/3
, and

\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-2}{3}-c)\rfloor}\frac{1}{3^{k-1}}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{k+1}(3j-2)(\frac{p-2}{3}-c)^{\underline{2k}}}{k!(c-1)^{\underline{2k+2}}}\neq 0.

We have checked this assumption in Magma
[
BCP
]
for all primes
p<2023
.

Theorem 7.15
.

The irreducible representation
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
of the rational Cherednik algebra
H_{1,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
over an algebraically closed field of characteristic
p>3
for
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
0<c<p/3
, is the quotient of the Verma module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
by the submodule generated by the vectors:

•

v_{p-3c}
in degree
p-3c
from Lemma
7.17
;

•

v_{+},v_{-}
in degree
p
from Lemma
6.7
;

•

v_{p+3c}
in degree
p+3c
from Lemma
7.19
.

Its character and Hilbert polynomial are

\displaystyle\chi_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)

\displaystyle=\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\left([\mathrm{Stand}]-[\mathrm{Triv}]z^{p-3c}-[\mathrm{Stand}]z^{p}-[\mathrm{Sign}]z^{3c+p}+[\mathrm{Stand}]z^{2p}\right)

\displaystyle h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{2-z^{p-3c}-2z^{p}-z^{3c+p}+2z^{2p}}{(1-z)^{2}}.

Proof.

The vectors
v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-}
and
v_{p+3c}
are described and proved to be singular in Lemmas
7.17
,
6.7
and
7.19
. After that, the strategy is to calculate the Hilbert polynomials of
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
and
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>
and show they are both equal to
\frac{2-z^{p-3c}-2z^{p}-z^{3c+p}+2z^{2p}}{(1-z)^{2}}
. The claim of the Theorem will then follow immediately.

Lemma
7.16
tells us that the module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
is an extension of the modules
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-kp]
with
k\geq 0
,
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})[-(kp-3c)]
with
k\geq 1
and
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(kp+3c)]
with
k\geq 0
. Consequently the Hilbert series of any quotient of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
will be a linear combination, with integer coefficients, of terms of the form
\frac{2z^{kp}}{(1-z)^{2}}
,
\frac{z^{kp-3c}}{(1-z)^{2}}
and
\frac{z^{kp+3c}}{(1-z)^{2}}
.

First, consider the degrees
0,1,\ldots,p-1
of
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
. Lemma
7.16
tells us that the only degrees among
0,1,\ldots,p-1
where
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
can have singular vectors and thus differ from
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
are
p-3c
and
3c
. Lemmas
7.17
and
7.18
(the latter using Assumption
7.14
) look for those singular vectors, finding one in degree
p-3c
and none in degree
3c
, and let us conclude that

h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)=\frac{1}{(1-z)^{2}}(2-z^{p-3c}+O(z^{p})).

(Here
O(z^{p})
denotes any sum of terms of degree greater or equal to
p
.)

This in turn lets us conclude that up to degree
p-1
, any quotient of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
looks either like
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
and has the Hilbert series
\frac{1}{(1-z)^{2}}(2+O(z^{p}))
or looks like
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
and has the Hilbert series
\frac{2-z^{p-3c}+O(z^{p})}{(1-z)^{2}}

Next, we turn our attention to
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>
. Lemma
7.20
shows that the quotient
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{p+3c}\right>
has the Hilbert series

h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{p+3c}\right>}(z)=\frac{1}{(1-z)^{2}}(2-z^{p-3c}-z^{p+3c}).

Lemma
7.21
then shows that
v_{+}
and
v_{-}
are not zero in
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{p+3c}\right>
. Thus, they generate a module
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
in degree
p
, which is a quotient of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
, and so, by the previous paragraph, up to degree
2p-1
looks either like
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-p]
with the Hilbert series
\frac{2z^{p}+O(z^{2p})}{(1-z)^{2}}
or like
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-p]
with the Hilbert series
\frac{2z^{p}-z^{2p-3c}+O(z^{2p})}{(1-z)^{2}}
. To see what the submodule
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c,p+3c}\right>
looks like in degrees up to
2p-1
, we notice it is the image of the map

\varphi:M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-p]\to M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c,p+3c}\right>

defined by
\varphi(b_{\pm})=v_{\pm}
. In Lemma
7.22
we show
\varphi(v_{p-3c})\neq 0
, which lets us conclude that
\left<v_{+},v_{-}\right>
has the Hilbert series
\frac{2z^{p}+O(z^{2p})}{(1-z)^{2}}
and that
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>
has the Hilbert series

(28)

h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>}(z)=\frac{1}{(1-z)^{2}}\left(2-z^{p-3c}-2\cdot z^{p}-z^{p+3c}+O(z^{2p})\right).

Lemma
7.23
further shows that degree
2p-1
of (
28
) is
0
, which lets us conclude that
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>
is concentrated in degrees up to
2p-2
, and has the Hilbert series

h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>}(z)=\frac{1}{(1-z)^{2}}\left(2-z^{p-3c}-2\cdot z^{p}-z^{p+3c}+2z^{2p}\right).

(The last statement follows because both are polynomials of degree
2p-2
, whose coefficients match up to degree
2p-1
).

We now return to
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
. It is a quotient of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+}v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>
by some submodule. This submodule is concentrated in degrees
p
and above by Lemmas
7.16
,
7.17
and
7.18
. Let
M
be the number of irreducible composition factors of this submodule of type
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-p]
, let
N
be the number of irreducible composition factors of this submodule of type
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})[-(2p-3c)]
and let
K
be the number of irreducible composition factors of this submodule of type
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(p+3c)]
. In the Grothendieck group,

\displaystyle[L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})]

\displaystyle=[M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>]-M\cdot[L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-p]]-

\displaystyle\quad-K\cdot[L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(3c+p)]]-N\cdot[L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})[-(2p-3c)]].

In terms of Hilbert polynomials

(29)

\displaystyle h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)

\displaystyle=h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>}(z)-M\cdot z^{p}\cdot h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)-

\displaystyle\quad-K\cdot z^{3c+p}\cdot h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})}(z)-N\cdot z^{2p-3c}h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})}(z).

Lemma
7.24
then lets us conclude that
M=K=N=0
, and that

L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})=M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>,

with the Hilbert series is as claimed. The character computation is direct.
∎

We will now state and prove all the lemmas used in the above proof.

Recall that Lemma
2.1

limits the degrees in which singular vectors can occur, because if
\tau
is an irreducible representation consisting of singular vectors in
M^{k}_{1,c}(\sigma)
or one of its quotients, then
\Omega|_{\tau}=\Omega|_{\sigma}+k
.
Thus, to describe all degrees
k
for which singular vectors of type
\tau
can occur in
M^{k}_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
or one of its quotients, we need to describe all
k\in\mathbb{N}
of the form
k=\Omega|_{\tau}-\Omega|_{\mathrm{Stand}}.
Their relative order is also important, as we look for singular vectors in order from smaller degrees to bigger, taking quotients each time and subsequently looking for singular vectors in the quotient. Putting that information together for
0<c<p/3
, we get the following lemma.

Lemma 7.16
.

If
\tau
is an irreducible representation consisting of singular vectors inside
M^{k}_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
or one of its quotients, then the pairs
(k,\tau)
are of the form:

(1)

For
c\in\left\{0,1,\ldots p-1\right\}
with
0<c<p/6
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}k:&\quad 3c<&p-3c<&p<&3c+p<&2p-3c<&\ldots\\
\tau:&\quad\mathrm{Sign}&\mathrm{Triv}&\mathrm{Stand}&\mathrm{Sign}&\mathrm{Triv}&\end{array}

(2)

For
c\in\left\{0,1,\ldots p-1\right\}
with
p/6<c<p/3
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}k:&\quad p-3c<&3c<&p<&2p-3c<&3c+p<&\ldots\\
\tau:&\quad\mathrm{Triv}&\mathrm{Sign}&\mathrm{Stand}&\mathrm{Triv}&\mathrm{Sign}&\end{array}

We will first examine the
\mathrm{Triv}
and
\mathrm{Sign}
isotypic components in degrees
p-3c
and
3c
.

Lemma 7.17
.

There is a one dimensional space of singular vectors in
M_{1,c}^{p-3c}(\mathrm{Stand})
, in the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component and spanned by
v_{p-3c}
given by:

(1)

If
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
,

\displaystyle v_{p-3c}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-1}{3}-c)\rfloor}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{9^{i}}\frac{(\frac{p-1}{3}-c)^{\underline{2i}}}{i!\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{i}(3j-2)}\sigma_{2}^{3i}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}-c-2i}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-1}{3}-c-1)\rfloor}\frac{(-1)^{i+1}}{6\cdot 9^{i}}\frac{(\frac{p-1}{3}-c)^{\underline{2i+1}}}{i!\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{i+1}(3j-2)}\sigma_{2}^{3i+1}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}-c-2i-1}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3\cdot(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right);

(2)

If
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
,

\displaystyle v_{p-3c}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-2}{3}-c-1)\rfloor}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{9^{i}}\frac{2(\frac{p-2}{3}-c)^{\underline{2i+1}}}{i!\prod_{j=1}^{i+1}(3j-1)}\sigma_{2}^{3i+2}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-2}{3}-c-2i-1}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-2}{3}-c)\rfloor}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{9^{i}}\frac{(\frac{p-2}{3}-c)^{\underline{2i}}}{i!\prod_{j=1}^{i}(3j-1)}\sigma_{2}^{3i}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-2}{3}-c-2i}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3\cdot(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right).

Proof.

By Theorem
2.6
, a basis for the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component of
M^{p-3c}_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
is

\left\{\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})\mid\quad 2a+3b=p-3c-1\right\}\,\,\cup

\cup\,\,\left\{\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3\cdot(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)\mid\quad 2a+3b=p-3c-2\right\}.

Using Section
5
, we calculate the values of
D_{y_{1}}
on this basis to be:

(30)

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}

\displaystyle(\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-}))=

\displaystyle=\frac{3c+1}{2}\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\otimes(b_{+}+3b_{-})+

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{b}{6}\sigma_{2}^{a+1}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\cdot\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}-3b_{-}\otimes b_{+}-3b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-3b_{-}\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{a}{12}\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}-3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}-\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.-3(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}-3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{b}{2}\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}q\otimes(b_{+}-b_{-});

(31)

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}

\displaystyle(\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3\cdot(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right))=

\displaystyle=-9a\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b+1}\otimes(b_{+}+3b_{-})+2b\sigma_{2}^{a+2}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\otimes(b_{+}+3b_{-})+

\displaystyle\quad+a\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}-3b_{-}\otimes b_{+}-3b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-3b_{-}\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{-b}{6}\sigma_{2}^{a+1}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}-3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}-\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.-3(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}-3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right)+

\displaystyle\quad+3a\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b}q\otimes(b_{+}-b_{-}).

We now consider the cases
p\equiv 1,2\bmod 3
separately to parametrize the integral solutions to
2a+3b=p-3c-1
and
2a+3b=p-3c-2
.

(1)

Assume
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
. A vector in the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component of
M^{p-3c}_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
is of the form

\displaystyle v_{p-3c}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-1}{3}-c)\rfloor}\lambda_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}-c-2i}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-1}{3}-c-1)\rfloor}\mu_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i+1}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}-c-2i-1}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3\cdot(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right).

Using equations (
30
) and (
31
), the condition
D_{y_{1}}(v_{p-3c})=0
leads to the following equations for the coefficients
\lambda_{i}
,
\mu_{i}
, which need to hold for all
i
:

(32)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}\cdot\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-1}{3}-c-2i)+\mu_{i}\cdot 3(3i+1)

\displaystyle=0

(33)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i+1}\cdot\frac{3(i+1)}{12}-\mu_{i}\cdot\frac{\frac{p-1}{3}-c-2i-1}{6}

\displaystyle=0

(34)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}\cdot\frac{3c+1}{2}+\mu_{i}\cdot(-9)(3i+1)+\mu_{i-1}\cdot 2(\frac{p-1}{3}-c-2i+1)

\displaystyle=0.

The system has a unique (up to overall scaling) solution

(35)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{i}}{9^{i}}\frac{(\frac{p-1}{3}-c)^{\underline{2i}}}{i!\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{i}(3j-2)}

\displaystyle\mu_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{i+1}}{6\cdot 9^{i}}\frac{(\frac{p-1}{3}-c)^{\underline{2i+1}}}{i!\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{i+1}(3j-2)}.

(2)

Assume
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
. A vector in the
\mathrm{Triv}
isotypic component of
M^{p-3c}_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
is of the form

\displaystyle v_{p-3c}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-2}{3}-c-1)\rfloor}\lambda_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i+2}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-2}{3}-c-2i-1}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-2}{3}-c)\rfloor}\mu_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-2}{3}-c-2i}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3\cdot(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\right).

Using equations (
30
) and (
31
), the condition
D_{y_{1}}(v_{p-3c})=0
leads to linear equations
with a unique (up to overall scaling) solution

(36)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{i}}{9^{i}}\frac{2(\frac{p-1}{3}-c)^{\underline{2i+1}}}{i!\prod_{j=1}^{i+1}(3j-1)}

\displaystyle\mu_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{i}}{9^{i}}\frac{(\frac{p-1}{3}-c)^{\underline{2i}}}{i!\prod_{j=1}^{i}(3j-1)}.

∎

Lemma 7.18
.

Assume
c
satisfies the conditions in Assumption
7.14
. Then
there are no singular vectors in
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})^{3c}/\left<v_{p-3c}\right>
.

Proof.

We distingush the cases
0<c<p/6
and
p/6<c<p/3
.

Case 1:
0<c<p/6
.
By Lemma
7.16

3c<p-3c
, so
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})^{3c}/\left<v_{p-3c}\right>=M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})^{3c}
, and it is enough to show there are no singular vectors in the
\mathrm{Sign}
component of
M_{1,c}^{3c}(\mathrm{Stand})
. This is the easier case as we are not working modulo
v_{p-3c}
. By Theorem
2.6
, any vector in the
\mathrm{Sign}
component of
M_{1,c}^{3c}(\mathrm{Stand})
can be uniquely expressed as

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(c-1)\rfloor}\nu_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-1-2i}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{+})+

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(c-2)\rfloor}\xi_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i+2}\sigma_{3}^{c-2-2i}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}-(2b_{+}b_{+})\otimes b_{+}\right).

We calculate that

(37)

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}

\displaystyle(\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{+}))=

\displaystyle=\frac{1-3c}{2}\cdot\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\otimes\left(b_{-}-b_{+}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{b}{6}\cdot\sigma_{2}^{a+1}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\cdot\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+b_{-}\otimes b_{+}+b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{a}{12}\cdot\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}+\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.+(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}-3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{b}{6}\cdot\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}q\otimes\left(3b_{-}-b_{+}\right),

(38)

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}

\displaystyle(\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}-(2b_{+}b_{+})\otimes b_{-}\right))=

\displaystyle=-9a\cdot\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b+1}\otimes\left(b_{-}-b_{+}\right)+2b\sigma_{2}^{a+2}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\otimes\left(b_{-}-b_{+}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+a\cdot\sigma_{2}^{a}\sigma_{3}^{b}\cdot\left(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+b_{-}\otimes b_{+}+b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{b}{6}\cdot\sigma_{2}^{a+1}\sigma_{3}^{b-1}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}+\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.+(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}-3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+a\cdot\sigma_{2}^{a-1}\sigma_{3}^{b}q\otimes\left(3b_{-}-b_{+}\right).

From here,
D_{y_{1}}(v)=0
gives the system

\displaystyle\frac{1-3c}{2}\cdot\nu_{i}-9(3i+2)\cdot\xi_{i}+2(c-2i)\cdot\xi_{i-1}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{c-1-2i}{6}\cdot\nu_{i}+(3i+2)\cdot\xi_{i}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\frac{3i+1}{12}\cdot\nu_{i}-\frac{c-2i}{6}\cdot\xi_{i-1}

\displaystyle=0,

the only solution to which is
\nu_{i}=0,\xi_{i}=0
for all
i
, so
v=0
.

Case 2:
p/6<c<p/3
.
This task is harder because
3c>p-3c
and we have to work modulo
v_{p-3c}
. We consider further two cases.

Case 2.1.
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
.
A vector in the
\mathrm{Sign}
component of
M^{3c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c}\right>
can be uniquely written as

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{c-1}{2}\rfloor}\nu_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-1-2i}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{+})+

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{\frac{p-1}{6}-\lfloor\frac{c-1}{2}\rfloor-2}\xi_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i+2}\sigma_{3}^{c-2-2i}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}-(2b_{+}b_{+})\otimes b_{-}\right)).

(Note the boundaries of summation, due to the fact that in
M(\mathrm{Stand})^{3c}/\left<v_{p-3c}\right>
the multiplicity of
\mathrm{Sign}
is
\frac{p-1}{6}
, and the above terms form a basis.)

Using Lemma
2.20
,
v
is singular in
M^{3c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c}\right>
if and only if
D_{y_{1}}(v)\in\left<v_{p-3c}\right>,
meaning that there exist
\pi_{j},\rho_{j}\in\Bbbk
such that

(39)

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(v)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p-1}{6}-1}\pi_{j}\sigma_{2}^{3j}\sigma_{3}^{2c-\frac{p-1}{3}-1-2j}v_{p-3c}(-b_{+}+b_{-})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p-1}{6}-1}\rho_{j}\sigma_{2}^{3j+1}\sigma_{3}^{2c-\frac{p-1}{3}-2-2j}v_{p-3c}(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})+(2b_{+}b_{-})).

Expanding this out using the explicit form for
v_{p-3c}
from Lemma
7.17
and equations (
37
) and (
38
) we get that (
39
) is equivalent to the following being satisfied for all
k
:

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{1-3c}{2}+\xi_{k}\cdot(-9)(3k+2)+

\displaystyle\xi_{k-1}\cdot 2(c-2k)=

(40)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad=\sum_{j=0}^{k}-6\pi_{j}\lambda_{k-j}+

\displaystyle 54\pi_{j}\mu_{k-j}+54\rho_{j}\lambda_{k-j}+72\rho_{j}\mu_{k-j}

(41)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{c-1-2k}{6}+\xi_{k}\cdot(3k+2)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k}-6\pi_{j}\mu_{k-j}-6\rho_{j}\lambda_{k-j}+108\rho_{j}\mu_{k-j}

(42)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{3k+1}{12}+\xi_{k-1}\cdot\frac{-c+2k}{6}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\frac{1}{2}\pi_{j}\lambda_{k-j}+6\rho_{j}\mu_{k-j-1}

(43)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{c-1-2k}{6}+\xi_{k}\cdot(3k+2)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k}-6\pi_{j}\mu_{k-j}+6\rho_{j}\lambda_{k-j},

where

\lambda_{i}
and
\mu_{i}
are as in Lemma
7.17
.

Subtracting equations (
41
) and (
43
) we get that for all
k

\sum_{j=0}^{k}\rho_{j}(-12\lambda_{k-j}+108\mu_{k-j})=0,

which (using the explicit form of
\lambda_{i}
and
\mu_{i}
from Lemma
7.17
to check that
-12\lambda_{k-j}+108\mu_{k-j}\neq 0
) implies that
\rho_{j}=0
for all
j
.

The system (
7.3
)-(
43
) is now equivalent to requiring that for all
k

(44)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{c-1-2k}{6}+\xi_{k}\cdot(3k+2)

\displaystyle=-6\sum_{j=0}^{k}\pi_{j}\mu_{k-j}

(45)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{3k+1}{12}+\xi_{k-1}\cdot\frac{-c+2k}{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\pi_{j}\lambda_{k-j}.

We split into two further cases.

Case 2.1.1. Assume
c
is even.
The relevance of this assumption is in the range of
k
for which the unknowns
\nu_{k},\xi_{k},\pi_{k}
and the parameters
\lambda_{k},\mu_{k}
are potentially nonzero. Namely, we are looking for all solutions to (
44
)-(
45
) where:

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\ldots 0\leq k\leq\frac{c}{2}-1

\displaystyle\xi_{k}\ldots 0\leq k\leq\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c}{2}-1

\displaystyle\pi_{k}\ldots 0\leq k\leq c-\frac{p-1}{6}-1

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}\ldots 0\leq k\leq\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c}{2}-1

\displaystyle\mu_{k}\ldots 0\leq k\leq\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c}{2}-1.

Let us prove by downwards induction on
m
that

\nu_{\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c}{2}+m}=0,\quad\pi_{m}=0\quad\textrm{ for all }m>0.

It is certainly true for large
m
(by convention). Assume it is true for
m+1,m+2,\ldots
, and let us prove it for
m
. Equation (
44
) for
k=\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c}{2}+m
reads
\nu_{\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c}{2}+m}=0
, and equation (
45
) for
k=\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c}{2}+m
implies
\pi_{m}=0
.

The system (
44
)-(
45
) is thus reduced to

(46)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{c-1-2k}{6}+\xi_{k}\cdot(3k+2)

\displaystyle=-6\pi_{0}\mu_{k}

(47)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{3k+1}{12}+\xi_{k-1}\cdot\frac{-c+2k}{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\pi_{0}\lambda_{k}.

We multiply (
46
) by
a_{k}
and (
47
) by
b_{k}
for

a_{k}=\frac{(-1)^{k+1}}{2}\frac{(3k+1)!}{k!(c-1)^{\underline{2k+1}}},\quad,b_{k}=(-1)^{k}\frac{(3k)!}{k!(c-1)^{\underline{2k}}},

and sum over all
k
to get, for
n=\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c}{2}
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\cdot\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(-6)\mu_{k}a_{k}+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{k}b_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\cdot\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\frac{-1}{2\cdot 3^{k}}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{k}(3j-1)(2n)^{\underline{2k+1}}}{k!(c-1)^{\underline{2k+1}}}+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{2\cdot 3^{k}}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{k}(3j-1)(2n)^{\underline{2k}}}{k!(c-1)^{\underline{2k}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\cdot\frac{c-\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{1}{2}}{c-1}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{3^{k}}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{k}(3j-1)(2n)^{\underline{2k}}}{k!(c-2)^{\underline{2k}}}.

As
\frac{c-\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{1}{2}}{c-1}\neq 0
for
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
p/6<c<p/3
, and as
\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{3^{k}}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{k}(3j-1)(2n)^{\underline{2k}}}{k!(c-2)^{\underline{2k}}}\neq 0
by Assumption
7.14
, we conclude that
\pi_{0}=0
. From here we proceed like in part (1) to deduce that
\nu_{k}=0
,
\xi_{k}=0
for all
k
, and
v=0
.

Case 2.1.2. Assume
c
is odd.
In this case we are looking for all solutions to (
44
)-(
45
) with the boundaries:

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\ldots 0\leq k\leq\frac{c-1}{2}

\displaystyle\xi_{k}\ldots 0\leq k\leq\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c-1}{2}-2

\displaystyle\pi_{k}\ldots 0\leq k\leq c-\frac{p-1}{6}-1

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}\ldots 0\leq k\leq\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c-1}{2}-1

\displaystyle\mu_{k}\ldots 0\leq k\leq\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c-1}{2}-1.

As before, we prove by downwards induction on
m
that

\nu_{\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c-1}{2}-1+m}=0\quad\pi_{m}=0\quad\textrm{ for all }m>0.

Assuming this holds for
m+1,m+2,\ldots,
we write equations (
44
)-(
45
) for
k=\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c-1}{2}-1+m
to get a system of two equations with two unknowns
\nu_{\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c-1}{2}-1+m}
and
\pi_{m}=0
, the only solution of which is
\nu_{\frac{p-1}{6}-\frac{c-1}{2}-1+m}=0
,
\pi_{m}=0
.

The remaining system is identical to the system (
46
)-(
47
), and we proceed as in case (i).

Case 2.2.
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
.
Similar. A vector in the
\mathrm{Sign}
component of
M_{1,c}^{3c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c}\right>
can be uniquely written as

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{c-1}{2}\rfloor}\nu_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i+1}\sigma_{3}^{c-1-2i}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{+})+

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{\frac{p+1}{6}-\lfloor\frac{c-1}{2}\rfloor-2}\xi_{i}\sigma_{2}^{3i+2}\sigma_{3}^{c-2-2i}\cdot\left((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}-(2b_{+}b_{+})\otimes b_{-}\right)).

Such a vector is singular in
M(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c}\right>
if and only if there exist
\pi_{j},\rho_{j}\in\Bbbk
such that

\displaystyle D_{y_{1}}(v)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{6}-1}\pi_{j}\sigma_{2}^{3j+1}\sigma_{3}^{2c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2j-1}v_{p-3c}(-b_{+}+b_{-})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=0}^{c-\frac{p+1}{6}-1}\rho_{j}\sigma_{2}^{3j+2}\sigma_{3}^{2c-\frac{p+1}{3}-2-2j}v_{p-3c}(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})+(2b_{+}b_{-})).

Reading off the coefficients we get a system of equations which lets us immediately conclude that
\rho_{j}=0
for all
j
. After this, the system is:

(48)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{c-1-2k}{6}+\xi_{k}\cdot(3k+2)

\displaystyle=-6\sum_{j=0}^{k}\pi_{j}\mu_{k-j}

(49)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{3k+1}{12}+\xi_{k-1}\cdot\frac{-c+2k}{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\pi_{j}\lambda_{k-j-1}.

As before, distinguishing the cases of even and odd
c
, we show by downwards induction on
m
that
\pi_{m}=0
and
\nu_{\frac{p+1}{6}-\frac{c}{2}-1+m}=0
for all
m>0
.

This leaves us with the system

(50)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{c-1-2k}{6}+\xi_{k}\cdot(3k+2)

\displaystyle=-6\pi_{0}\mu_{k}

(51)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}\cdot\frac{3k+1}{12}+\xi_{k-1}\cdot\frac{-c+2k}{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\pi_{0}\lambda_{k-1},

which can be shown by telescoping to have no nonzero solutions precisely when Assumption
7.14
is satisfied. We thus conclude that
v=0
.
∎

Next, as dictated by the proof of Theorem
7.15
, we search
M^{p+3c}_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
for singular vectors of type
\mathrm{Sign}
.

Lemma 7.19
.

The vector
v_{p+3c}\in M^{p+3c}_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
given below is singular.

(1)

If
{{p}\,\equiv\,{1}\>(\mathrm{mod}\>3)}
,

\displaystyle v_{p+3c}=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-1}{3}+c)\rfloor}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{9^{i}}\frac{(\frac{p-1}{3}+c)^{\underline{2i}}}{i!\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{i}(3j-2)}\sigma_{2}^{3i}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}+c-2i}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{+})+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-1}{3}+c-1)\rfloor}\frac{(-1)^{i+1}}{6\cdot 9^{i}}\frac{(\frac{p-1}{3}+c)^{\underline{2i+1}}}{i!\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{i+1}(3j-2)}\sigma_{2}^{3i+1}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}+c-2i-1}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\cdot\bigg((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3\cdot(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}\bigg);

(2)

If
{{p}\,\equiv\,{2}\>(\mathrm{mod}\>3)}
,

\displaystyle v_{p+3c}=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-2}{3}+c-1)\rfloor}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{9^{i}}\frac{2(\frac{p-2}{3}+c)^{\underline{2i+1}}}{i!\prod_{j=1}^{i+1}(3j-1)}\sigma_{2}^{3i+2}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-2}{3}+c-2i-1}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{+})+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\frac{p-2}{3}+c)\rfloor}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{9^{i}}\frac{(\frac{p-2}{3}+c)^{\underline{2i}}}{i!\prod_{j=1}^{i}(3j-1)}\sigma_{2}^{3i}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-2}{3}+c-2i}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\cdot\bigg((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{-}-(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{+}\bigg).

Proof.

The proof, very similar to the proofs of Lemmas
7.18
and
7.19
, is a direct computation using formulas (
37
) and (
38
), showing that
D_{y_{1}}(v_{p+3c})=0.

∎

After this, as outlined in the proof of Theorem
7.15
, in the next lemmas we calculate the Hilbert polynomial of the quotient of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
by
\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>
.

Lemma 7.20
.

The Hilbert polynomial of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c,v_{p+3c}}\right>
equals

h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{p+3c}\right>}(z)=\frac{1}{(1-z)^{2}}(2-z^{p-3c}-z^{p+3c}).

Proof.

It is straightforward to see that
\left<v_{p-3c}\right>\cong M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})[-(p-3c)]
and
\left<v_{p+3c}\right>\cong M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})[-(p+3c)]
, so we only need to calculate Hilbert series of
\left<v_{p-3c}\right>\cap\left<v_{p+3c}\right>
. This is done similar to the proof of Lemma
7.11
, or Lemma
6.12
and
6.14
.

Write

\displaystyle v_{p-3c}

\displaystyle=f\otimes b_{+}+g\otimes b_{-}

\displaystyle v_{p+3c}

\displaystyle=h\otimes b_{+}+k\otimes b_{-}

with
f,g,h,k\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.
A vector is in
\left<v_{p-3c}\right>\cap\left<v_{p-3c}\right>
if and only if it can be written as

Av_{p-3c}=Bv_{p+3c}

for some
A,B\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. This leads to the system

(52)

\displaystyle Af-Bh

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle Ag-Bk

\displaystyle=0,

and to see if this has any nontrivial solutions, we will calculate its determinant
fk-gh.

First, the facts that
v_{p-3c}
is
S_{3}
invariant and that
v_{p+3c}
is
S_{3}
anti-invariant lead to

\displaystyle(12).f

\displaystyle=f,

\displaystyle(12).g

\displaystyle=-g,

\displaystyle(12).h

\displaystyle=-h,

\displaystyle(12).k

\displaystyle=k

\displaystyle(23).f

\displaystyle=\frac{-f+g}{2},

\displaystyle(23).g

\displaystyle=\frac{3f+g}{2},

\displaystyle(23).h

\displaystyle=\frac{h-k}{2},

\displaystyle(23).k

\displaystyle=\frac{-3h-k}{2}

\displaystyle(13).f

\displaystyle=\frac{-f-g}{2},

\displaystyle(23).g

\displaystyle=\frac{-3f+g}{2},

\displaystyle(23).h

\displaystyle=\frac{h+k}{2},

\displaystyle(23).k

\displaystyle=\frac{3h-k}{2}.

Next, the fact that
v_{p-3c}
and
v_{p+3c}
are both singular leads to

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{1}}(f)

\displaystyle=-c\frac{1}{x_{1}-x_{3}}\frac{3f+g}{2}

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{1}}(g)

\displaystyle=-2c\frac{g}{x_{1}-x_{2}}-c\frac{1}{x_{1}-x_{3}}\frac{3f+g}{2}

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{1}}(h)

\displaystyle=c\frac{2h}{x_{1}-x_{2}}+c\frac{1}{x_{1}-x_{3}}\frac{h-k}{2}

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{1}}(k)

\displaystyle=-3c\frac{1}{x_{1}-x_{3}}\frac{h-k}{2}.

From here it is straightforward to show that
fk-gh
is an
S_{3}
invariant with the property
D_{y_{1}}(fk-gh)=0
, so
\partial_{y}(fk-gh)=0
for all
y
and thus
fk-gh
is a
p
-th power and an invariant. Given that its degree is
2p
, we conclude that
fk-gh
is a scalar multiple of
\sigma_{2}^{p}
.

To calculate this scalar, we calculate
fk-gh
explicitly, using formulas in Lemmas
7.17
and
7.19
, while disregarding all terms with a nonzero power of
\sigma_{3}
(as we know from the above that they don’t contribute to the final expression as
fk-gh
is a scalar multiple of
\sigma_{2}^{p}
). We distinguish four cases, and claim that in all of them
fk-gh\neq 0
.

(1)

If
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
and
m=\frac{p-1}{3}-c
is even, then
\frac{p-1}{3}+c=m+2c
is even and we get

\displaystyle v_{p-3c}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{m/2}}{9^{m/2}}\frac{m!}{(m/2)!\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{m/2}(3j-2)}\sigma_{2}^{3m/2}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+\sigma_{3}\ldots

\displaystyle v_{p+3c}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{m/2+c}}{9^{m/2+c}}\frac{(m+2c)!}{(m/2+c)!\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{m/2+c}(3j-2)}\sigma_{2}^{3(m/2+c)}\cdot(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{+})+\sigma_{3}\ldots

\displaystyle fk-gh

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{m/2}}{9^{m/2}}\frac{m!}{(m/2)!\prod_{j=1}^{m/2}(3j-2)}\frac{(-1)^{m/2+c}}{9^{m/2+c}}\frac{(m+2c)!}{(m/2+c)!\prod_{j=1}^{m/2+c}(3j-2)}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sigma_{2}^{3m/2}\sigma_{2}^{3(m/2+c)}(b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{\frac{p-1}{3}}(-12)({\frac{p-1}{3}}-c)!({\frac{p-1}{3}}+c)!}{9^{\frac{p-1}{3}}(\frac{1}{2}({\frac{p-1}{3}}-c))!(\frac{1}{2}({\frac{p-1}{3}}+c))!\prod_{j=1}^{\frac{1}{2}({\frac{p-1}{3}}-c)}(3j-2)\prod_{j=1}^{\frac{1}{2}({\frac{p-1}{3}}+c)}(3j-2)}\sigma_{2}^{p}\neq 0.

(2)

If
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
and
m=\frac{p-1}{3}-c
is odd - similar.

(3)

If
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
and
m=\frac{p-2}{3}-c
is even - similar.

(4)

If
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
and
m=\frac{p-2}{3}-c
is odd - similar.

This proves that
fk-gh\neq 0
, which implies that the only solution to the system (
52
) is
A=B=0
, so
\left<v_{p-3c}\right>\cap\left<v_{p-3c}\right>=0
. Consequently,

\displaystyle h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{p+3c}\right>}(z)

\displaystyle=h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)-h_{\left<v_{p-3c}\right>}(z)-h_{\left<v_{p+3c}\right>}(z)+h_{\left<v_{p-3c}\right>\cap\left<v_{p-3c}\right>}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(1-z)^{2}}-z^{p-3c}\cdot\frac{1}{(1-z)^{2}}-z^{p+3c}\cdot\frac{1}{(1-z)^{2}}+0.

∎

Lemma 7.21
.

The images of the vectors
v_{+},v_{-}
from Lemma
6.7
in the quotient module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{p+3c}\right>
are nonzero.

Proof.

Let us show that
v_{+}
is not contained in
\left<v_{p-3c},v_{p+3c}\right>
.
As
v_{-}
can be calculated from
v_{+}
by the action of
\Bbbk(S_{3})\subseteq H_{1,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
and
\left<v_{p-3c},v_{p+3c}\right>
is an
H_{1,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
submodule, so the claim for
v_{-}
will follow. Further, noting that
\deg v_{+}=p>p-3c=\deg v_{p-3c}
and
\deg v_{+}=p<p+3c=\deg v_{p+3c}
, the claim is equivalent to showing that
v_{+}\notin\left<v_{p-3c}\right>
.

Assume the contrary, that
v_{+}\in\left<v_{p-3c}\right>
. In that case there exists
A\in S^{3c}\mathfrak{h}^{*}
such that
v_{+}=Av_{p-3c}
. Considering how these vectors transform under the action of
S_{3}
(
v_{p-3c}
spans
\mathrm{Triv}
and
v_{+}
spans the
(12)
-invariant part of
\mathrm{Stand}
), we can see that
A
needs to transform like the
S_{2}
-invariant part of
\mathrm{Stand}
and thus needs to be of the form

A=gb_{+}+h(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})

for some
g\in(S^{3c-1}\mathfrak{h}^{*})^{S_{3}}
,
h\in(S^{3c-2}\mathfrak{h}^{*})^{S_{3}}
. We will consider the equation
v_{+}=Av_{p-3c}
modulo
\sigma_{2}
to show it has no solutions. For that purpose, we distinguish two cases.

(1)

If
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
, then

\displaystyle v_{+}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}}(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+\sigma_{2}\ldots

\displaystyle v_{p-3c}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}-c}(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+\sigma_{2}\ldots.

Modulo
\sigma_{2}
, the equation
Av_{p-3c}=v_{+}
becomes

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}}(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})

\displaystyle=(gb_{+}+h(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2}))\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}-c}(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+\sigma_{2}\ldots

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}^{c}(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})

\displaystyle=(gb_{+}^{2}+h(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\cdot b_{+})\otimes b_{+}+

\displaystyle+(3gb_{+}b_{-}+3h(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\cdot b_{-})\otimes b_{-}+\sigma_{2}\ldots

\displaystyle=(\frac{-1}{2}g(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})+h\cdot 54\sigma_{3})\otimes b_{+}+

\displaystyle+(\frac{1}{2}g(2b_{+}b_{-})+3h\cdot(-6q))\otimes b_{-}+\sigma_{2}\ldots.

Reading off the coefficient of
(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})
we get

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}^{c}

\displaystyle=0

which is a contradiction.

(2)

If
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
, then

\displaystyle v_{+}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-2}{3}}(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-})+\sigma_{2}\ldots

\displaystyle v_{p-3c}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-2}{3}-c}((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-})+\sigma_{2}\ldots

The equation
Av_{p-3c}=v_{+}
, similar to above, becomes

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}^{c}(-(-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-})

\displaystyle=(g\cdot 54\sigma_{3}+h\cdot(-108)\sigma_{3}b_{+})\otimes b_{+}+

\displaystyle+3(g\cdot 6q+h\cdot 108\sigma_{3}b_{-})\otimes b_{-}+\sigma_{2}\ldots

leading to

\sigma_{3}^{c}=0
which is a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 7.22
.

Let

\varphi:M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-p]\to M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{p+3c}\right>

be the map of
H_{1,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
-modules given by
\varphi(f\otimes b_{\pm})=f\cdot v_{\pm}
. Then
\varphi(v_{p-3c})\neq 0
.

Proof.

Let
\psi:M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})[-p]\to M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
be the map of
H_{1,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
modules given by the same formula. Then
\varphi(v_{p-3c})=0
if and only if there exist
A,B\in S\mathfrak{h}^{*}
such that

\psi(v_{p-3c})=A\cdot v_{p-3c}+B\cdot v_{p+3c}.

Considering how they transform under
S_{3}
, we get that
A\in(S\mathfrak{h}^{*})^{S_{3}}
and
B=B^{\prime}q
for
B^{\prime}\in(S\mathfrak{h}^{*})^{S_{3}}
. We then calculate modulo
\sigma_{2}
, using the explicit expressions for
v_{\pm},v_{p-3c},v_{p+3c}
from Lemmas
7.17
,
6.7
and
7.19
, and distinguishing two cases.

(1)

If
p\equiv 1\bmod 3
, then

\displaystyle v_{+}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}}(-b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})\bmod\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle v_{-}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}}(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}+b_{-}\otimes b_{+})\bmod\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle v_{p-3c}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}-c}(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})\bmod\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle v_{p+3c}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}+c}(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-3b_{-}\otimes b_{+})\bmod\sigma_{2}.

We calculate modulo
\sigma_{2}
:

\displaystyle\psi(v_{p-3c})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}-c}(b_{+}\cdot v_{+}+3b_{-}\cdot v_{-})\bmod\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{3}^{2\frac{p-1}{3}-c}((-b_{+}^{2}+3b_{-}^{2})\otimes b_{+}+3(2b_{+}b_{-})\otimes b_{-})\bmod\sigma_{2}.

At the same time, for any
A,B^{\prime}\in(S\mathfrak{h}^{*})^{S_{3}}
we have

\displaystyle A\cdot v_{p-3c}+B^{\prime}\cdot qv_{p+3c}

\displaystyle=A\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}-c}(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})+

\displaystyle\quad+B^{\prime}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}+c}q(b_{+}\otimes b_{-}-b_{-}\otimes b_{+})\bmod\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(A\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}-c}-3B^{\prime}\sigma_{3}^{\frac{p-1}{3}+c+1}\right)(b_{+}\otimes b_{+}+3b_{-}\otimes b_{-})\bmod\sigma_{2}.

Comparing these expressions we see that there for all
A,B

\psi(v_{p-3c})\neq A\cdot v_{p-3c}+B\cdot v_{p+3c},

so
\varphi(v_{p-3c})\neq 0
as claimed.

(2)

If
p\equiv 2\bmod 3
- similar.

∎

Finally, the end of the proof of Theorem
7.15
requires some facts about Hilbert polynomials, which we prove in the last two Lemmas of this subsection.

Lemma 7.23
.

The coefficient of
z^{2p-1}
in the expansion of

\frac{1}{(1-z)^{2}}\left(2-z^{p-3c}-2\cdot z^{p}-z^{p+3c}\right)

around
z=0
is
0
.

Proof.

The coefficient of
z^{k}
in
\frac{1}{(1-z)^{2}}
is
k+1
, so the coefficient of
z^{2p-1}
in the above expression is

\displaystyle 2(2p-1+1)-(2p-1-(p-3c)+1)-2(2p-1-p+1)-(2p-1-(p+3c)+1)=0.

∎

At this point in the proof of Theorem
7.15
we conclude that the Hilbert polynomial of the irreducible module
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
has the form (
29
). The following Lemma shows that the only module with such Hilbert polynomial is
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>
itself.

Lemma 7.24
.

Let
Q
be a quotient of
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>
with a Hilbert series of the form

\displaystyle h_{Q}(z)

\displaystyle=h_{M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>}(z)-M\cdot z^{p}\cdot h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)-

\displaystyle\quad-K\cdot z^{3c+p}\cdot h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})}(z)-N\cdot z^{2p-3c}h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Triv})}(z)+O(z^{2p})

for some
M,N,K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then:

(1)

M=0
;

(2)

K=N=0
;

(3)

Q=M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>
.

Proof.

(1)

We distinguish two cases.

Case 1. If
0<c<p/6
,
calculate the dimension of
Q^{2p-3c}
. We have
p+3c<2p-3c
, so

\displaystyle\dim{Q^{2p-3c}}

\displaystyle=\dim{M_{1,c}^{2p-3c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>}-

\displaystyle-M\dim{L_{1,c}^{p-3c}(\mathrm{Stand})}-K\dim{L_{1,c}^{p-6c}(\mathrm{Sign})}-N\dim{L_{1,c}^{0}(\mathrm{Triv})}.

By equation (
28
) we know

\displaystyle\dim

\displaystyle(M_{1,c}^{2p-3c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>)=

\displaystyle\quad=2(2p-3c+1)-(2p-3c-(p-3c)+1)-

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-2(2p-3c-p+1)-(2p-3c-(p-3c)+1)

\displaystyle\quad=6c-2.

Equation (
29
) lets us conclude that

\displaystyle\dim{L_{1,c}^{p-3c}(\mathrm{Stand})}

\displaystyle=\dim M_{1,c}^{p-3c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>

\displaystyle=2(p-3c+1)-1=2p-6c+1.

By Theorem
7.1

h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Sign})}(z)=\left(\frac{1-z^{p-3c}}{1-z}\right)^{2}
so

\dim{L_{1,c}^{p-6c}(\mathrm{Sign})}=p-6c+1.

We also know that
\dim{L_{1,c}^{0}(\mathrm{Triv})}=1
.
Putting it all together we get:

\displaystyle 0\leq\dim{Q^{2p-3c}}

\displaystyle=(6c-2)-M\cdot(2p-6c+1)-K\cdot(p-6c+1)-N\cdot 1.

Rearranging and using
c<p/6
we get

\displaystyle p-2

\displaystyle>6c-2

\displaystyle\geq M\cdot(2p-6c+1)+K\cdot(p-6c+1)+N

\displaystyle\geq M\cdot(2p-6c+1)

\displaystyle>M\cdot(p+1).

The only nonnegative integer
M
satisfying this is
M=0
.

Case 2.
p/6<c<p/3
.
We have
2p-3c<p+3c
and similarly calculate the dimension of
Q^{p+3c}
, getting:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\dim{Q^{p+3c}}=\dim{M_{1,c}^{p+3c}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>}-

\displaystyle\quad-M\cdot\dim{L_{1,c}^{3c}(\mathrm{Stand})}-K\cdot\dim{L_{1,c}^{0}(\mathrm{Sign})}-N\cdot\dim{L_{1,c}^{6c-p}(\mathrm{Triv})}

\displaystyle=\left(2\cdot(p+3c+1)-(p+3c-p+3c+1)-2(p+3c-p+1)-1\right)-

\displaystyle\quad-M\cdot\left(2\cdot(3c+1)-(3c-p+3c+1)\right)-K\cdot 1-N\cdot(6c-p+1)

\displaystyle=\left(2p-6c-2\right)-M\cdot\left(p+1)\right)-K-N\cdot(6c-p+1)

\displaystyle\leq\left(2p-6c-2\right)-M\cdot\left(p+1\right).

We rearrange this as

M\cdot\left(p+1\right)\leq 2p-6c-2<p+6c-6c-2=p-2,

and notice that the only nonnegative integer
M
satisfying this is
M=0
.

(2)

Using part (1), we calculate the dimension of
Q^{2p-1}
as

\displaystyle\dim{Q}^{2p-1}

\displaystyle=\dim{M_{1,c}^{2p-1}(\mathrm{Stand})/\left<v_{p-3c},v_{+},v_{-},v_{p+3c}\right>}-

\displaystyle\quad-K\cdot\dim{L_{1,c}^{p-3c-1}(\mathrm{Sign})}-N\cdot\dim{L_{1,c}^{3c-1}(\mathrm{Triv})}

\displaystyle=\left(2\cdot 2p-(2p-1-p+3c+1)-2(2p-1-p+1)-(2p-1-p-3c+1)\right)-

\displaystyle\quad-K\cdot(p-3c-1+1)-N\cdot(3c-1+1)

\displaystyle=-K\cdot(p-3c)-N\cdot 3c.

Using that
\dim{Q}^{2p-1}\geq 0
,
p-3c>0
and
3c>0
, the only nonnegative
K,N
satisfying this are
K=N=0.

(3)

This follows immediately from (1) and (2).

∎

7.4.
Irreducible representation
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
, part 2

Conjecture 7.25
.

The irreducible representation
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
of the rational Cherednik algebra
H_{1,c}(S_{3},\mathfrak{h})
over an algebraically closed field of characteristic
p>3
for
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
p/3<c<p/2
, is the quotient of the Verma module
M_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
by the submodule generated by vectors in the following degrees:

•

-p+3c
- one dimensional space, of type
\mathrm{Sign}

•

3p-3c
- one dimensional space, of type
\mathrm{Triv}

•

p+3c
- one dimensional space, of type
\mathrm{Sign}

•

5p-3c
- one dimensional space, of type
\mathrm{Triv}

Its character and Hilbert polynomial are

\displaystyle\chi_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)

\displaystyle=\chi_{S\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(z)\left([\mathrm{Stand}]-z^{-p+3c}[\mathrm{Sign}]-z^{3p-3c}[\mathrm{Triv}]-\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.-z^{p+3c}[\mathrm{Sign}]-z^{5p-3c}[\mathrm{Triv}]+z^{4p}[\mathrm{Stand}]\right)

\displaystyle h_{L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{2-z^{-p+3c}-z^{3p-3c}-z^{p+3c}-z^{5p-3c}+2z^{4p}}{(1-z)^{2}}.

Compare this with Lemma
3.19
, where an analogous result is proved for
p=2
. Notice also that the vectors
v_{+},v_{-}
from Lemma
6.7
, which are singular in the generic case, are in this case contained in the submodule generated in degrees
-p+3c
and
3p-3c
so do not have to be included in the list of generators of the maximal proper submodule here.

We have checked this conjecture using Magma
[
BCP
]
for all
p\leq 13
and all
c
, and a small number of larger
p
and specific
c
. For example, when
p=13
and
c=6
,
L_{1,c}(\mathrm{Stand})
has
50
nonzero graded pieces and dimension
886
.
